BASELINE CHANGE #1  --  2026-08-21 18:05
================================================================================
PROMOTED: --init zero  (E17).  Baseline is now wnotap + --init zero.
Rationale (section 6 SIMPLER): held-out 6.3686 at 30K against wnotap's 6.3635 at
40K -- equal loss in 10K fewer steps -- and it REMOVES the random state seed.
Equivalence plus simplicity is a promotion, not a tie.  Promoted on a
mismatched-step comparison (30K vs 40K) rather than spending an hour to match
them; recorded here so the weaker evidence is visible.

NEW BASELINE COMMAND (exp/run.sh now emits this):
  python vla.py --dataset web --preroll --tokenizer pythia31m \
    --context 16 --n_hidden 256 --kernel 3 \
    --batch 250 --monitor 20 --checkpoint 5000 \
    --opt sgd --momentum 0.0 --weight_decay 0.0 \
    --schedule const --lr_max 0.01 --seed 1 \
    --n_layers 7 --depth 7 --clock fixed --clock_base 2.36 \
    --init zero --ema 0.5 --accumulate
  Weights: checkpoint/BASELINE_init0_30k.pt (30K steps).

BASELINE NUMBERS -- carry these forward, the section 2 wnotap figures are now
the OLD baseline and must not be quoted in new predictions:
  train mean(last10) @30K   6.1685
  held-out clean     @30K   6.3686   median 6.3524   runaway 0.0%
  gen cycle          @30K   0.08
  crossings: L<7.549 1340   L<7.0 4360   L<6.5 14400
  KNOWN: this configuration passed through a total collapse at 20K (100% runaway,
  gen cycle 0.77) and recovered by 25K.  Expect excursions; do not terminate on
  one reading.

QUEUE RE-EXPRESSED against the new baseline (section 4 rule):
  DROPPED  E02 --init relu    -- superseded; --init zero won and relu was the
                                 weaker version of the same idea
  DROPPED  E17               -- it is the baseline now
  E01 result restated: --ema 0.25 was measured against the OLD baseline.  Its
    reach deficit (+0.0245 vs +0.0756) may or may not survive --init zero, since
    the seed it was damping is gone.  Not re-queued; noted as unresolved.
  All remaining entries (E03 --no-accumulate, E05 --schedule cosine,
    E06 --batch 500, E09 --qproj read, E11 --lr_max 0.02, E12 --clock incr,
    E15/E16 shape) are still exactly ONE change from the new baseline and stand.

E18  --no-accumulate   budget 30K   NEXT
  Chosen for coverage (6c: zero data on this dimension on web) AND because it is
  a REMOVAL (section 6 SIMPLER corollary: a tie still advances the design).
  What --accumulate does: a clocked layer commits the MEAN of every output it
  computed since its last write, instead of the single output from the step it
  writes on.  At clock_base 2.36 the deepest layer holds 173 steps, so it is
  averaging up to 173 outputs -- heavy smoothing, and one extra DFF-sized buffer
  per layer, which is real memory on an FPGA.
  Hypothesis: that averaging destroys the fine temporal detail the slow layers
  exist to carry.  Sampling one output instead may sharpen them.
  Predict: noisier early loss, equal or better by 30K, reach equal or better.
  Falsified if: loss clearly worse at 20K AND 30K, i.e. the mean is doing real
  denoising that a single sample cannot replace.
  If it TIES: promote it.  It removes a buffer per layer and a mechanism.

  DECISION @30K: TERMINATE E18 (--no-accumulate).  REJECTED.
  Held-out clean across every checkpoint, vs baseline 6.3686 @30K:
      10K 8.3255   15K 8.2993   20K 8.5051   25K 8.6120   30K 9.2343
  It was never good -- 1.9 to 2.9 nats worse than baseline at every point, and
  ABOVE the 7.549 unigram floor, while its TRAINING loss read 6.36.  Degrades
  monotonically after 15K.  Runaway 0.0% throughout, so nothing is diverging;
  it is uniformly bad on every document (median == clean).
  The pre-registered falsifier ("loss clearly worse at 20K AND 30K") fired.
  --accumulate is doing real denoising that a single sample cannot replace, the
  opposite of the hypothesis that averaging 173 outputs destroys fine detail.
  PARTIAL EXPLANATION, not established: training loss is computed in TRAIN mode
  (BatchNorm batch statistics over 250 rows) while score.py runs in EVAL mode
  (running statistics).  E18's state swung dff_std 0.44 -> 2.83 -> 0.49 and
  dff_max 49 -> 388 -> 8, so BatchNorm's ~10-step running statistics were chasing
  a moving target.  But held-out was already 8.33 at 10K when dff_std was 1.77,
  so "unstable state breaks BN" cannot be separated from "--no-accumulate is
  simply bad".  Left as a hypothesis.
  LESSON REINFORCED: a 2.9-nat train/held-out gap was invisible in training loss,
  gradient, dff_max, dff_zeros AND generation health (cycle stayed 0.08-0.10).
  Only the held-out eval-mode score saw it.  Score every experiment.

  NOTE on --accumulate storage (user asked): it IS a second buffer per layer,
  self.acc[li] = torch.zeros_like(o), same shape as the register, not an in-place
  accumulation on the DFF.  It has to be: the register holds the COMMITTED value
  between writes because _fb() reads it as feedback every step, so if the
  register accumulated the running sum the layer would no longer be holding,
  which is the whole point of the clock.  --ema is the in-place variant and needs
  no buffer.  For an FPGA the accumulator is only needed for layers with period
  > 1, and being a plain sum of ReLU outputs divided once it could be lower
  precision or integer.

OVERNIGHT RESULTS  2026-08-21 21:39 -> 2026-08-22 05:44
Baseline: wnotap + --init zero + --accumulate.  All four runs clean: 0.0% runaway,
generation cycle 0.08-0.10.
================================================================================
  run            change                 train@20K   held-out   verdict
  e19_lr02       --lr_max 0.02            6.2332     6.3681    WINS
  e20_3x17       3x17 clock_base 13.15    6.3552     6.3677    TIES
  (baseline)     --                       6.3730     6.3686 @30K
  e21_17x3       17x3 clock_base 1.38     6.6004     6.6937    LOSES
  e22_batch500   --batch 500 (10K steps)  6.5177*    6.6091    LOSES
  * token-matched: 10K steps at batch 500 = 5.0M tokens = 20K at batch 250

E19 --lr_max 0.02: ahead at EVERY checkpoint (5K 6.797 vs 6.926, 10K 6.491 vs
  6.627, 15K 6.340 vs 6.470, 20K 6.233 vs 6.373).  Held-out 6.3681 at 20K equals
  the baseline's 6.3686 at 30K -- same loss in 10K FEWER steps.  Confirms the
  suspicion recorded in 6c: lr 0.01 was chosen from a 200-STEP grid and is too
  low for 20K+ horizons.  Every comparison in this study before now inherited it.
E20 3x17 TIES the baseline's 7x7.  Three timescales with 17 convs between them
  performs identically to seven timescales with 7 convs.
E21 17x3 LOSES badly (+0.325 held-out).  Each layer gets 3 convs, fewer than the
  ~15 needed to mix a 16-wide grid within one layer, so layers are spatially
  local and mixing happens only through the stack.
  TOGETHER E20/E21 answer the partition question asymmetrically: going COARSER
  is free, going FINER is expensive.  7x7 was never justified but is not wrong;
  3x17 is equally good and simpler (3 registers instead of 7, 3 accumulator
  buffers instead of 7) -- a SIMPLER-rule promotion candidate if confirmed.
E22 --batch 500 is worse per token AND costs 2x compute per step.  Batch 250
  stands.  Dimension closed.

NEXT: E23 --lr_max 0.04.  Only one side of the lr optimum is bracketed -- 0.02
  beat 0.01 at every checkpoint, so the optimum may be higher still.  Falsifier:
  worse than 0.02 at 10K AND 20K.  Watch dff_max: the 200-step grid had lr 0.1
  at dff_max 6e5, so instability is expected somewhere between 0.02 and 0.1.

12. DAY PLAN  2026-08-22 10:00 -> 22:00  (UNATTENDED, no user feedback available)

Baseline at start of day: wnotap + --init zero + --accumulate, lr TBD by phase 1.
  python vla.py --dataset web --preroll --tokenizer pythia31m \
    --context 16 --n_hidden 256 --kernel 3 --batch 250 --monitor 20 \
    --checkpoint 5000 --opt sgd --momentum 0.0 --weight_decay 0.0 \
    --schedule const --lr_max <best> --seed 1 \
    --n_layers 7 --depth 7 --clock fixed --clock_base 2.36 \
    --init zero --ema 0.5 --accumulate

CONTEXT: lr is the dominant lever found so far and EVERY experiment before
2026-08-21 21:39 ran at 0.01, which came from a 200-STEP grid.  Measured:
  lr 0.01  train@20K 6.3730   lr 0.02  6.2332   lr 0.04  6.372 at 10K already
Each doubling has won.  The optimum is still above 0.04.

PHASE 1  SETTLE LR  (2-3 runs, adaptive, ~4-6 h)
  E24 --lr_max 0.08, 20K.
  RULE (apply without asking): keep doubling while BOTH
      (a) the higher lr has lower train loss at 10K, and
      (b) dff_max stays under 1000 through the run.
  Stop at the first doubling that fails either test.  Then, if the failure was
  large, probe the midpoint between the last winner and the loser.
  The 200-step grid had lr 0.1 at dff_max 6e5, so the edge is expected between
  0.04 and 0.16; phase 1 should terminate in 2 runs.
  ABORT a run if train loss is still above 7.549 (unigram floor) at 10K.

PHASE 2  3x17 AT THE BEST LR  (1 run, 20K, ~2 h)
  --n_layers 3 --depth 17 --clock_base 13.15
  E20 tied 7x7 at lr 0.01 and is SIMPLER: 3 DFF registers and 3 accumulator
  buffers instead of 7, a direct FPGA saving.  Tests whether the tie composes
  with the new lr.  A TIE PROMOTES IT (section 6 SIMPLER).

PHASE 3  --ema 0.25 AT THE BEST LR  (1 run, 20K, ~2 h)
  E01 measured this against the OLD gaussian-init baseline and PLAN.txt records
  the result as UNRESOLVED: the random seed it was damping no longer exists.
  Re-run it against the current baseline.

PHASE 4  --clock exp AT THE BEST LR  (1 run, 20K, ~2 h)
  The clock has never been retested since --tap was removed.  exp won on
  TinyStories and lost on web WITH the tap; the tap is gone, so the comparison
  is void and the dimension is effectively uncovered.

PHASE 5  BEST CONFIG TO 40K + REACH PROBE  (1 run, ~2 h + 20 min)
  Confirms the lr advantage persists past 20K, and re-measures reach, which has
  NOT been checked since the baseline changed to --init zero.  Reach is a GATE
  (section 6c): mean excess must stay above ~+0.04.  Reference: wnotap +0.0756,
  wfix236 +0.0359, wexp +0.0023 (short-reach failure).

STANDING RULES WHILE UNATTENDED
  - Score EVERY final checkpoint held-out.  E18 proved a 2.9-nat train/held-out
    gap can be invisible to training loss, gradient, dff_max, dff_zeros AND
    generation health simultaneously.
  - Never terminate on a single bad reading (section 3e).  Excursions are
    universal and have recovered every time they were given 5-10K more steps.
  - Report and decide at every 5K checkpoint into PLAN.txt (section 11).
  - If an experiment is inconclusive, move to the next phase rather than
    extending -- coverage beats depth (section 6c), and there is no one to ask.
  - Prefer the SIMPLER configuration on any tie (section 6).

e23_lr04.final  20000   9.8150   9.8960      11.8    47    531       9.847

  E23 --lr_max 0.04: REJECTED.  BEST training loss in the study, BROKEN held-out.
      train@20K 6.1329 (vs 0.02 -> 6.2332, 0.01 -> 6.3730)
      held-out  9.8150, median 9.8960, runaway 11.8% (47/400), first bad pos 531
  9.815 is ABOVE the 7.549 unigram floor: on held-out documents it is worse than
  a frequency table, while its training curve was the best ever recorded here.
  The lr doubling ladder crossed a stability edge between 0.02 and 0.04 and the
  training loss concealed it completely -- the pre-registered rule would have run
  0.08 next.  PHASE 1 RULE AMENDED: the doubling test must use HELD-OUT loss, not
  train loss at 10K.  Bisecting with lr 0.03 instead of doubling.
  WINNER SO FAR: lr 0.02, held-out 6.3681 @20K == baseline 6.3686 @30K, 0.0%
  runaway.  All downstream phases pinned to 0.02.

  PATTERN, now twice in two days (E18 --no-accumulate, E23 --lr_max 0.04):
  a configuration can have BETTER training loss than baseline and be 2.9-3.4 nats
  worse held-out, with training loss, gradient, dff_max, dff_zeros AND generation
  health all clean.  Only the held-out eval-mode score detects it.  It costs ~3
  minutes.  Score every checkpoint, without exception.

  DIRECTIVE (user, 2026-08-22): 3x17 IS HIGHLY DESIRABLE.  If it comes back
  MARGINALLY worse, do not reject it -- try to recover it with the learning rate.
  Why this is not a violation of the one-change rule: optimal lr is a property of
  the architecture, not a free parameter, so comparing two architectures at a
  single lr tuned for one of them is the confounded comparison, not the fix.
  3x17 has 17 convs per ContextCNN against 7x7's 7, so the gradient reaching each
  layer differs in scale; there is no reason the same lr is right for both.
  Why 3x17 is worth spending runs on: 3 DFF registers instead of 7, and 3
  accumulator buffers instead of 7 (see the --accumulate storage note).  On an
  FPGA that is a direct halving of the state memory and the buffer count, which
  no loss number captures.  It also has 3 timescales (ladder 1,13,173) instead of
  7, i.e. a much simpler clock.
  RULE: if 3x17 at the best lr is within ~0.15 held-out of the baseline, run it
  again at a different lr before deciding.  Try LOWER first (0.015): more convs
  per layer means a longer gradient path within each ContextCNN, which usually
  wants a smaller step.  If that does not recover it, try 0.03.
  Only reject 3x17 if it loses at its OWN best lr.
  Budget: this may cost 1-2 extra runs.  Drop PHASE 4 (--clock exp) to pay for
  it if needed -- the clock dimension is less valuable than a 2x state reduction.

lr 0.03 beats 0.02 by 0.120 held-out (outside sigma) with zero runaway, and leads
at every checkpoint from 5K.

THE STABILITY EDGE IS BETWEEN 0.03 AND 0.04 -- a factor of 1.33.  This is the
most practically useful result of the day: the usable lr band is very narrow, and
CROSSING THE EDGE IMPROVES TRAINING LOSS (0.04 has the best train@20K of any run
here, 6.1329) while destroying the model held-out.  Nothing but an eval-mode
held-out score distinguishes the two sides.  Anyone tuning this architecture must
know this or they will tune straight off the cliff.

QUEUE RE-EXPRESSED (section 4 rule): all remaining phases re-pinned from lr 0.02
to lr 0.03.  exp/run.sh updated so the baseline emits 0.03.  Phase 2 (3x17) was
started at 0.02 and has been killed and requeued at 0.03 -- comparing 3x17 at a
stale lr against a 0.03 baseline would have been exactly the confounded
comparison the 3x17 directive warns about.
NOTE for later: 0.035 is untested and the edge is close; if a future run wants
the last bit of lr, bisect 0.03-0.04 rather than assuming 0.03 is optimal.

   5    e28_long      baseline to 40K      6.1311    0.0%    BEST MODEL

E26 --ema 0.25 RESOLVES the item PLAN.txt flagged as unresolved after E01 (E01
  measured it against the OLD gaussian-init baseline, and the seed it was damping
  no longer existed).  Against the current baseline it is 0.088 WORSE with 6.2%
  runaway.  SETTLED: keep --ema 0.5.  The --ema dimension is closed.
E27 --clock exp fails by 3.4 nats, above the 7.549 unigram floor, with 0.0%
  runaway -- uniformly poor rather than diverging.  It WON on TinyStories and
  loses badly on web.  Combined with E21 (17x3) and E20/E25 (3x17 ties then
  wins), the clock dimension is now CLOSED: --clock fixed with a base tuned so
  p_max ~ 173 is correct on web.  Any TinyStories clock conclusion should be
  treated as void, not merely unconfirmed.
E28 40K at the promoted config: held-out 6.1311, the best model of the study.
  For scale, wnotap reached 6.0805 at 100K -- roughly equal quality at 40% of the
  tokens.  Training loss curve was still descending at 40K.

REACH GATE at 40K (e28_long), warm-context spliced probe:
  P= 64  mean +0.0744   P=512 mean +0.0341
  c32 +0.137  c64 +0.116  c96 +0.070  c128 +0.050  c192 +0.047  c256 +0.074
  c320 +0.020  c384 +0.029  c448 +0.043  c511 +0.006
  PASSES the ~+0.04 gate and is document-scale, comparable to wfix236's +0.0359.
  CAVEAT: the spliced probe reports 4.8-7.4% runaway ROWS, so the curve is
  computed on the surviving ~93%.  The per-document score on the same checkpoint
  is 0.0% runaway, so this is the harsher mixed-context test, not a broken model.

CURRENT BEST CONFIGURATION (exp/run.sh emits this):
  python vla.py --dataset web --preroll --tokenizer pythia31m \
    --context 16 --n_hidden 256 --kernel 3 --batch 250 --monitor 20 \
    --checkpoint 5000 --opt sgd --momentum 0.0 --weight_decay 0.0 \
    --schedule const --lr_max 0.03 --seed 1 \
    --n_layers 3 --depth 17 --clock fixed --clock_base 13.15 \
    --init zero --ema 0.5 --accumulate
  Weights: checkpoint/e28_long.final.pt is 40K at 7x7; the 3x17 promotion has
  NOT yet been run past 20K.  checkpoint/e25_3x17.final.pt is 3x17 @20K.

DIMENSIONS NOW CLOSED (do not re-open without a reason)
  --clock       fixed wins; exp and incr lose on web
  --ema         0.5; 0.25 is worse
  --accumulate  keep it; --no-accumulate is catastrophic held-out
  --tap         off; removing it improved both loss and reach
  --init        zero; gaussian is worse and is a needless mechanism
  --batch       250; 500 is worse per token and costs 2x compute
  --lr_max      0.03; the stability edge is between 0.03 and 0.04
  shape         3x17 >= 7x7 >> 17x3; coarser is free, finer is expensive

STILL UNCOVERED (candidates for the next session)
  --n_hidden    256 in every run ever.  The only untested capacity knob that is
                not confounded by receptive field (--context is: at 3x3 pad=1
                the RF covers the grid only up to context ~50).
  --kernel      3 in every run ever.  kernel 5 doubles RF growth per conv.
  --schedule    const in every run ever.  Horizon-bound, so it belongs LAST,
                applied once to the settled architecture at the real horizon.
  --qproj read  never tested.  Re-introduces an unnormalized map on the DFF path,
                which is what removing --tap eliminated -- watch dff_max.
  --clock_base  13.15 was derived to hold p_max=173 at L=3, never optimized at
                L=3.  p_max itself is untested at the new shape.
  3x17 past 20K.  The promotion rests on a 20K comparison.

================================================================================
13. WEIGHT DECAY STUDY  --  CANCELLED, superseded by section 14 (--norm none)
================================================================================
Runs after E30.  Capacity (--n_hidden, --kernel) is deferred to LAST by user
direction, so weight decay is the next dimension.

WHY IT IS INTERESTING HERE, AND WHY THE OBVIOUS ARGUMENT IS WRONG:
ContextCNN is Conv -> BatchNorm2d(affine=False) -> ReLU throughout, and weight
decay on a conv that is immediately BatchNormed does NOT shrink the function --
BN renormalizes the scale away, so decay only changes the EFFECTIVE learning
rate.  That is the standard result and it would make this study pointless...
except that this architecture has convs with NO normalization after them:
    text_proj    (c_text -> n_hidden, 1x1, the token injection)
    deep_proj    (n_hidden -> n_hidden, 1x1, between stacked layers)
    decoder.proj (n_hidden -> c_text, 1x1, the readout)
Those three are the only unnormalized maps left after --tap was removed, and
deep_proj sits directly on the feedback path.  Weight decay genuinely shrinks
them.  So this measures something real and specific: what happens when the
unnormalized parts of the recurrence are pulled toward zero.

SECOND REASON, from the E30 result: the loss has FLATTENED (5.580 -> 5.562 ->
5.569 across 80K-120K) with dff_max steady at 10-13.  A converged run is exactly
where a regularizer can show a benefit that a still-descending run would hide.

BASELINE for this study: 3x17, lr 0.03, --init zero, --ema 0.5, --accumulate,
weight_decay 0.0.  Reference numbers come from E29/E30.

  E31  --weight_decay 1e-4   20K   the conservative point
  E32  --weight_decay 1e-3   20K   10x
  E33  --weight_decay 1e-2   20K   100x; expected to be too strong

PREDICT: 1e-4 indistinguishable, 1e-3 the interesting one, 1e-2 clearly worse.
  If any value wins, the mechanism is the three unnormalized 1x1 convs, and the
  follow-up is to decay ONLY those (would need a new flag, e.g. --wd_proj) --
  which would be a cleaner intervention than global decay, since decaying the
  BatchNormed convs is just an lr change in disguise and lr is already tuned.
FALSIFIER: all three within sigma of baseline, i.e. weight decay does nothing
  here because the BN-invariant convs dominate the parameter count (3x17 has 51
  BatchNormed convs at 590K each against 3 unnormalized 1x1s at 66K each -- the
  decayable fraction is only ~0.6% of parameters).  That is the most likely
  outcome and worth recording either way.
NOTE: because weight decay under BN acts as an effective-lr change, a wd result
  must be read against the KNOWN lr sensitivity: lr 0.03 is 0.12 better than
  0.02 and 0.04 is BROKEN.  A wd that helps may simply be nudging the effective
  lr, so any winner must be re-checked at a slightly different lr before being
  attributed to regularization.

================================================================================
14. NO-BATCHNORM STUDY  (user-directed, 2026-08-23).  vla.py commit eced110.
================================================================================
Weight decay is CANCELLED.  The reason it looked interesting was that only three
convs in the model are unnormalized, so decay could act on them specifically --
but that is a ~0.6% slice of the parameters.  Removing BatchNorm attacks the same
structure from the other side and is far more consequential.

--norm none: ContextCNN becomes Conv2d -> ReLU.  He init (kaiming_normal, fan_in,
relu gain) replaces PyTorch's default, which has a gain far below 1 for ReLU and
would shrink the signal ~3x per layer -- over 51 layers that reaches the readout
as nothing.  Verified: block = [Conv2d, ReLU], ZERO buffers (vs 34 running-stat
entries per block), same parameter count, in std 1.01 -> out 0.65 over 2 layers
(BatchNorm: 0.99 -> 0.58), so the init preserves variance comparably.

WHY THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT REMAINING EXPERIMENT
BatchNorm is the ONLY module here whose function differs between train and eval,
and the recurrence amplifies that difference every step.  Every pathology in this
study traces to it:
  - --no-accumulate: train 6.36, held-out 9.23 (2.9 nats), all other metrics clean
  - --lr_max 0.04:   best training loss ever recorded here, held-out 9.82 (3.4)
  - free-running generation locking into a repeating token while training-time
    metrics stayed clean (genstate.py showed the state growing 10x per 5 tokens)
  - the runaway excursions, where the state amplifies under FROZEN running stats
--norm none makes train and eval the same function by construction, so these are
removed rather than managed.  It also deletes the running-statistics buffers,
which are real state in an FPGA dataflow.

RISK, stated plainly: BatchNorm is what makes a 51-conv chain trainable at all.
A plain deep chain may simply fail to train, or need a much lower lr.  That is
the experiment.

DESIGN (user, 2026-08-23): focus on the INITIAL STEPS TO BREAKTHROUGH, and try
momentum and especially ADAMW.  Rationale for both:
  - Breakthrough is make-or-break for a no-BN deep chain.  Every run in this study
    crosses the 7.549 unigram floor between 1.2K and 6K steps; if --norm none is
    going to fail, it fails there.  So the grid is SHORT runs (10K) scored on
    steps-to-breakthrough, not long runs scored on final loss.
  - ADAMW IS THE INTERESTING ONE.  AdamW failed badly WITH BatchNorm: it parked at
    the unigram floor for thousands of steps with dff_max reaching 3.7e5.  The
    mechanism was BN scale-invariance -- BN makes the conv weights' scale
    irrelevant to the function, so those directions are loss-neutral, and AdamW's
    per-parameter normalization promotes exactly the loss-neutral directions to
    full-size lr steps.  Remove BN and that pathology has no cause.  AdamW may
    now be the better optimizer, and it was rejected on evidence that no longer
    applies.
  - Momentum was likewise only ever tested with BN present.

GRID, 10K each (~55 min), all --norm none at the promoted 3x17 baseline.
ORDERED MOST-NOVEL-FIRST: the adamw and momentum runs can OVERTURN a dimension
this study closed on day one, while the sgd points mostly confirm what is known.
If time runs short, sacrifice the sgd tail, not the head.
  E31  --opt sgd   --lr_max 0.03                     the current optimizer
  E32  --opt sgd   --lr_max 0.01                     no-BN nets often want less
  E33  --opt sgd   --lr_max 0.1                      BN's scale-invariance imposed
                                                     an lr ceiling; without it the
                                                     usable range may be higher
  E34  --opt sgd   --lr_max 0.01 --momentum 0.9 --nesterov
  E35  --opt adamw --lr_max 0.001 --beta1 0.9 --beta2 0.999
  E36  --opt adamw --lr_max 0.0003 --beta1 0.9 --beta2 0.999
TRAP: exp/run.sh passes --momentum 0.0, and for adamw --momentum is the --beta2
  FALLBACK.  An adamw run without an explicit --beta2 would get betas (0.9, 0.0),
  which is not Adam at all.  The adamw entries above pass both betas explicitly.
SCORE: steps to sustained L<7.549 and L<7.0, loss at 10K, dff_max, and whether it
  breaks through at all.  Held-out score on every final checkpoint as usual.
CHARACTERIZE, not just score: report the train/held-out GAP explicitly for E31
  against the baseline's, plus generation health and reach.  The gap is the
  quantity this experiment exists to measure.

E30 FINAL -- BEST MODEL OF THE STUDY  (3x17 + BatchNorm, 140K steps, 35M tokens)
================================================================================
  held-out clean 5.6617   median 5.6430   runaway 0.0%   gen cycle 0.08
  weights: checkpoint/e30_3x17_120k.final.pt

  model                     held-out   steps   tokens
  e30_3x17 (this)            5.6617     140K   35.0M
  wnotap (old baseline)      6.0805     100K   25.0M
  e28_long (7x7, lr 0.03)    6.1311      40K   10.0M
  wexp (first web run)       6.3483      20K    5.0M

REACH -- the strongest measured in this study:
  P= 64 mean +0.2107 (0.0% runaway)   P=512 mean +0.1286 (0.0% runaway)
  c32 +0.203  c64 +0.189  c96 +0.127  c128 +0.153  c192 +0.117  c256 +0.089
  c320 +0.072  c384 +0.078  c448 +0.098  c511 +0.073
  Mean excess +0.1286 against wnotap's +0.0756 and wfix236's +0.0359, and still
  paying +0.07 to +0.10 at c=384-511 where every earlier model had decayed to
  ~0.00-0.05.  This model uses context FIVE HUNDRED tokens back on 890-token
  documents.  First reach probe in the study with 0.0% runaway ROWS at both
  periods, so the curve is computed on everything, not a surviving subset.

REVERSES AN EARLIER CONCLUSION: the reach/loss trade-off documented in section 3c
("on TinyStories, reach costs loss"; wexp had the best loss and a ~10-token
reach) does NOT hold for the promoted configuration.  E30 improved reach by 70%
over the previous best WHILE improving loss by 0.42 nats.  The trade-off appears
to have been a property of the tapped, gaussian-init, lr-0.01 regime rather than
of the architecture.  Treat section 3c as superseded.

CONVERGENCE: training loss flattened at ~5.60 over 120K-132K (5.569, 5.617,
5.592, 5.620 -- no downward trend).  More tokens on THIS configuration buy
nothing; the remaining directions are structural (--norm none, then capacity).

  E35 --norm none + AdamW 1e-3 + --clip_dff 100: REJECTED, never broke through.
      step      loss     grad    dff_max   dff_zeros
         0   10.8260    0.73     100.00    0.5361
      2000    7.6350    1.74      52.95    0.7947
      6000    7.5542    1.44      46.98    0.8781   best, still above 7.549
      8000    7.9108    0.04     100.00    0.9099   gradient collapses
     10000    7.8485    0.03     100.00    0.9270
  FAILURE MODE IS DYING RELUS, NOT ADAMW.  dff_zeros climbs monotonically
  0.54 -> 0.93: without BatchNorm nothing re-centres the pre-activations, the
  ReLUs progressively die, and by 10K 93% of the state is exactly zero.  grad
  collapses 1.7 -> 0.03 (the drowned-token signature, section 6b).  dff_max pins
  at exactly 100.00 for the last three checkpoints, so the clip binds
  continuously -- the surviving ~7% of units saturate against the ceiling while
  the rest are dead.  A degenerate state, not a working one.
  THIS DOES NOT YET VINDICATE OR CONDEMN ADAMW: the failure is a normalization
  problem that any optimizer would face.  E36/E34/E31-33 will show whether ANY
  optimizer trains a 51-conv chain with no normalizer.
  ALSO NOTE --clip_dff behaved correctly and is now load-bearing rather than a
  patch: it capped the step-0 write at 100 instead of the 4.46e5 latched without
  it, a 4500x reduction in what the 173-step layer had to shed.

  MECHANISM FOUND EN ROUTE (user spotted it): dff_max halves on a fixed period.
  --ema 0.5 makes each write register <- 0.5*register + 0.5*new, so a write
  HALVES a register holding a huge stale value.  dff_max is dominated by the
  slowest layer, which at 3x17 / clock_base 13.15 writes every 173 steps.
  Measured ratio exactly 0.50 per 173 steps, with flat plateaus in between where
  that layer had not written.  This is the clock working as designed, made
  visible because the held value was 6 orders of magnitude too large.

================================================================================
PROCESS FIX  2026-08-23 12:05  -- after a 90-minute silent GPU stall
================================================================================
WHAT HAPPENED: exp/nobn.sh was edited WHILE RUNNING to insert E37/E38 ahead of
the SGD tail.  Bash reads a script incrementally by byte offset; the insertion
shifted every later offset, bash resumed at the shifted position, and it executed
the OLD E31/E32/E33 lines instead of the inserted ones.  It then printed
"NOBN GRID COMPLETE" and exited.  The session was waiting on a watcher for E37,
which had never been queued, so nothing noticed and the GPU sat idle 10:36-12:03.
This failure mode was already known in this session -- phase1.sh was deliberately
NOT edited while running, for exactly this reason -- and the lesson was not
carried forward.

FIX 1: exp/launch.sh <driver.sh> [args]
  Copies the driver to exp/.run/<name>.<timestamp>.sh and execs the SNAPSHOT.
  The running job can no longer be affected by edits to the source, and the
  snapshot is a permanent record of exactly what was executed.
  USE THIS FOR EVERY DRIVER FROM NOW ON.  To change a queue mid-flight, edit the
  source and relaunch, or write a NEW driver -- never edit the live one.

FIX 2: exp/idle_watch.sh, armed as a persistent Monitor
  Emits a notification after 5 consecutive minutes with no `python vla.py`
  running, then every 15 minutes while it persists, along with the last line of
  each driver log so the cause is visible immediately.
  This is the general fix: it catches ANY stall -- driver exited early, trainer
  crashed, chained job whose predecessor never signalled, a waiter armed on a
  filename that does not exist -- not just the edit-a-running-script bug.
  A stalled GPU is the one failure that costs time silently; everything else
  produces a wrong number that gets caught by the next score.

NOT WASTED: the accidental E31/E32/E33 runs completed the optimizer picture for
--norm none.  Every SGD variant NaNs within 4-8 steps (E31 lr 0.03 step 7,
E32 lr 0.01, E33 lr 0.1 step 4, E34 momentum step 8), while AdamW survives.
So plain SGD cannot train this architecture without a normalizer at ANY lr
tried, and AdamW's per-parameter normalization is doing part of the job
BatchNorm used to.

BASELINE CHANGE #4  --  2026-08-23 20:30
================================================================================
PROMOTED: --no-bn_track  (B1).  Baseline is now 3x17 + lr 0.03 + --init zero +
--accumulate + --no-bn_track.
                        train@20K   held-out   median   runaway   gen cycle
  b1_notrack             6.0000      6.2227    6.2071     0.8%      0.10
  baseline (track on)    6.1450      6.2203      --       0.0%      0.09
Held-out differs by 0.0024 -- a tie.  Promoted on section 6 SIMPLER:
  train == eval BY CONSTRUCTION (verified exactly; the tracked version has an
    11x std mismatch after only three layers), which structurally removes the
    mechanism behind every train/held-out blowup recorded in this file
  ZERO running-statistics buffers (1539 per ContextCNN gone) -- real state on the
    FPGA target
  no parameter change, no runtime change
Weights: checkpoint/BASELINE_notrack_20k.pt

THIS ANSWERS THE QUESTION THE WHOLE DAY WAS CHASING.  Removing normalization
failed six ways (every SGD lr NaN'd in 3-8 steps; AdamW either killed the ReLUs
or did not learn).  Replacing it with per-sample normalizers failed nine more
(GroupNorm at 4 optimizer settings, Softmax2d on the state, standardization on
the write and read paths at 3 lrs) -- all sat at the unigram floor with the
gradient suppressed to 0.15-1.7.  What works is keeping BatchNorm's CROSS-BATCH
pooling over the 250 document streams and simply not tracking running statistics.
The train/eval discrepancy was never inherent to BatchNorm, only to the running
averages.

CAVEAT to verify, not assume: gencheck runs at batch 1, where BatchNorm2d
degenerates to InstanceNorm over the SxS grid -- a different function from what
trained at batch 250.  cycle 0.10 says generation survives it, but long-form
generation deserves a closer look before this is relied on.

TOOL BUG FIXED (same session): exp/score.py forwarded only clock_* and clip_dff
from saved_args, so it rebuilt every --norm/--bn_* checkpoint with DEFAULT
normalization and failed to load it.  Those score calls died silently into a
grep, which is why today's --norm none / GroupNorm runs have no held-out numbers.
It does not change their rejections -- none crossed the unigram floor on TRAINING
loss, which is sufficient -- but the gap is real.  score.py now forwards every
architecture key.  reach.py still needs the same fix.

  DECISION RULE FOR TONIGHT (B2, B4 -- --bn_affine), stated in advance:
    promote only if held-out beats the baseline by MORE than 0.07.
    a tie or anything worse REJECTS it: --bn_affine adds 2*n_hidden per layer
    (~26K over 51 layers) and it must pay for that, not merely match.
  Baselines to compare against:
    B2 ran against the OLD baseline (tracked BN): held-out 6.2203
    B4 runs against the PROMOTED baseline (--no-bn_track): held-out 6.2227
  NOTE --bn_affine changes the state_dict (adds weight/bias per BatchNorm), so
  unlike --no-bn_track it cannot be toggled on an existing checkpoint.  If B4
  wins, B5's long run would have to be redone on top of it rather than resumed.
  ALSO: on torch 2.8.0 BatchNorm2d has NO separate `bias` argument -- affine=True
  creates weight AND bias together, so these runs test the pair.  Newer torch
  adds `bias` to separate them.  If --bn_affine wins, isolating which half is
  responsible would need a manual module here.  Worth suspecting the BIAS half:
  every block is Conv->BN->ReLU, so beta shifts the pre-activation relative to
  ReLU's threshold and controls what fraction of units fire -- and dead units
  were the dominant failure mode all day.  gamma is partly redundant with the
  following conv's weights.

============================================================================
2026-08-24 07:05  HORIZON VALIDATION (H1) -- METHOD FINDING
============================================================================
RESULT THAT FORCED THIS.  Head-to-head at matched 100K, scored in one process:

    e30_3x17_120k.100000   100000   5.7567   5.7407   runaway 0.1%
    b5_long.final          100000   5.7781   5.7588   runaway 0.0%

A tie.  The same b5 weights scored 5.7528 (night3) and 5.7781 (this run) on
two scorer seeds -- a 0.025 spread -- so 0.021 is not a difference.

Therefore: FOUR promotions (lr 0.03, 3x17, --init zero, --no-bn_track), each
of which won its own 20K head-to-head, NET TO ZERO at 100K.  Training loss
shows b5 ahead to 60K and e30 ahead at 80-100K (5.6123 vs 5.6410), i.e. the
crossover happens PAST the horizon every one of those decisions was made at.

Neither model is converged: e30 gained 0.095 going 100K -> 140K.

CONSEQUENCE.  Roughly a dozen decisions in this study assume the 20K ranking
predicts the 100K ranking.  That assumption is now measured to be false at
least once, so it cannot be relied on anywhere without evidence.  This is a
method defect, not a model defect, and it outranks any pending E-list item.

H1 = the cheapest experiment that produces evidence, because it does double
duty.  b4 (--bn_affine) is identical to b5 except for the one flag, and b5's
curve is already measured at 20/40/60/80/100K, so no control run is needed.
b4 was REJECTED last night on a 20K held-out tie (6.1948 vs 6.2227) despite
leading on EVERY training checkpoint with a margin that GREW to 0.069 by 20K.

PRE-REGISTERED READING (fixed before the run, b4 vs b5 held-out at 80K):
  * b4 ahead by >0.07 -> --bn_affine is real, the 20K rejection was a horizon
    artifact, and every 20K decision needs re-examination.
  * tie (|d| <= 0.07) -> rejection STANDS on the simplicity rule (affine adds
    ~26K params), and 20K was an adequate horizon in this case.
  * b4 behind         -> rejection stands outright, 20K was fine.
Both checkpoints are scored in ONE process so the 0.025 seed spread cancels.

REACH, for the record (b5 passed the gate but is the weaker long-range model):
             mean excess   c128    c256    c384    c511
    b5         +0.0948    +0.183  +0.078  +0.047  -0.068
    e30        +0.1286    +0.153  +0.089  +0.078  +0.073
b5 clears the +0.04 gate, but decays to NEGATIVE excess at c511 while e30
holds +0.073.  b5's advantage is concentrated at c96-c128 and gone by c384.
So on the reach REQUIREMENT e30 is better, while loss is tied.  Neither is
promoted: they differ by one real thing (tail reach) that no experiment in
the list was designed to test.

TOOLING.  exp/idle_watch.sh counted only "python vla.py" as busy, so the
reach probe read as a 20-minute idle GPU and fired a false alarm.  It now
counts score/reach/gencheck/memtest/genstate too, and reports the 4 most
recent driver logs instead of four hardcoded stale ones.

============================================================================
2026-08-24 07:25  B5 PROMOTED TO BASELINE.  H1 TERMINATED.  B6 = REPRO RUN.
============================================================================
CORRECTION FIRST.  I reported that e30 and b5 differed by four promotions
(lr 0.03, 3x17, --init zero, --no-bn_track) and that those four "net to zero
at 100K".  That was wrong -- inferred from the promotion history instead of
read from the checkpoints.  Diffing saved_args shows e30 ALREADY had lr 0.03,
3x17 and --init zero.  The two models differ by EXACTLY ONE flag:

    arg          E30                    B5
    bn_track     True (default then)    False      <-- the only difference
    (n_layers 3, depth 17, lr_max 0.03, sgd/mom 0.0, init zero, ema 0.5,
     accumulate, clock fixed base 13.15, context 16, n_hidden 256, kernel 3,
     batch 250, preroll -- all IDENTICAL)

Confirmed in the weights: e30 has 102 running_* buffers (56.14M params),
b5 has 0 (56.11M).  51 BatchNorms x 2 buffers = the whole delta.

So the e30-vs-b5 comparison is a clean single-variable test of --no-bn_track,
and the corrected finding is narrower than what I claimed: ONE 20K win became
a 100K tie on loss with worse tail reach.  Still evidence that the 20K horizon
is unreliable, but one instance, not a four-way collapse.

DECISION (user).  B5 is the new baseline.  exp/run.sh already emitted exactly
this config, so the promotion is a comment change; the config of record is:
  --dataset web --preroll --tokenizer pythia31m --context 16 --n_hidden 256
  --kernel 3 --batch 250 --opt sgd --momentum 0.0 --weight_decay 0.0
  --schedule const --lr_max 0.03 --n_layers 3 --depth 17 --clock fixed
  --clock_base 13.15 --init zero --ema 0.5 --accumulate --no-bn_track

H1 (--bn_affine to 80K) TERMINATED at step 23180, unscored.  Its pre-registered
reading is void; --bn_affine remains rejected on the 20K result.  The horizon
question it was built to answer is NOT resolved -- and B6 now speaks to it more
directly anyway, because a repro run measures the noise floor that every
promotion (including the 20K ones) has been compared against.

B6 = B5 rerun FROM SCRATCH at --seed 2, 100K steps (~9 h, ETA ~16:45).
Rationale: every decision here compares two runs, so the run-to-run sigma AT
100K is the bar each future decision must clear, and it has only ever been
estimated (~0.07) at short horizons.  --seed sets weight init only; the data
worker is unseeded, so B6 differs in init AND example order -- total variance,
which is the quantity that matters for ranking configs.
Caveat: b5 reached 100K as 20K + 80K resumed, b6 runs 100K in one shot.  If
they land far apart, check "resumed vs from-scratch" before blaming the seed.
Reference: b5 held-out 5.7528/5.7781, train @20/40/60/80/100K =
6.1523 5.8963 5.7810 5.6966 5.6410, reach +0.0948 (c511 -0.068).

============================================================================
2026-08-24 11:15  BIGRAM FLOOR OF OPENWEBTEXT  (exp/ngram.py)
============================================================================
Measured on EXACTLY the documents exp/score.py evaluates (openwebtext
train[-3000:], minlen 512, maxlen 1024, first 400 docs = 353,934 tokens) and
over exactly the same (x,y) pairs, because clip_text emits (input,target) so
P(y|x) IS a bigram.  Fit set is a disjoint slice, train[:N].  Interpolated
with unigram, P(y|x) = L*c(x,y)/c(x) + (1-L)*Puni(y); L tuned ON the held-out
set, which makes the bigram number OPTIMISTIC on purpose -- an optimistic
floor is the conservative choice when the argument is "the model must beat
this".  Sanity check: held-out unigram 7.5732 doc-mean vs 7.5491 on record.

  fit tokens   distinct bigrams   seen%   bigram nats (doc mean)   median
     8.5M           1.98M         81.3%        6.0619            5.9778
    16.9M           3.21M         85.5%        5.9186            5.8481
    28.3M           4.55M         88.1%        5.8279            5.7552
    33.8M           5.11M         88.9%        5.8027            5.7313
    56.6M           7.16M         90.9%        5.7311            5.6735
    67.6M           8.02M         91.6%        5.7087            5.6515
   113.2M          11.15M         93.3%        5.6485            5.5899
   226.5M          17.14M         95.1%        5.5830            5.5380

MODEL, for comparison (held-out, same docs):
    b5_long.final  100K steps   5.7528 / 5.7781 (two scorer seeds)  med 5.7396
    e30 @140K                   5.6617                              med 5.6430

TWO READINGS, BOTH TRUE, AND THE DIFFERENCE MATTERS.

(1) At a MATCHED DATA BUDGET the model is ahead, but barely.  b5 saw ~25M
    tokens (batch 250 x 100K steps x 1 token/step).  A bigram fit on 28.3M
    tokens scores 5.8279; b5 scores 5.7528.  The model is worth ~0.075 nats
    over a bigram table trained on the same amount of text.  That is a real
    but very small margin -- about the size of the run-to-run sigma we just
    measured (0.02-0.035 held-out, excursions to 0.067).

(2) At UNLIMITED DATA the bigram wins outright.  226M tokens (2.5% of
    openwebtext) gives 5.5830, beating b5 (5.7528) AND e30@140K (5.6617),
    and it is still falling ~0.065 per doubling with no sign of a floor.

So: 100K steps of a 30M-parameter recurrent CNN produce a model that a bigram
count table matches on 10x the data and beats on 25x.  The reach probe already
said this -- context is worth +0.09 nats, i.e. ~5% of the 1.8 nats the model
has captured below unigram -- and the generated text says it too: correct
function-word rates, correct capitalization and numeral classes, occasional
2-3 word islands, no clause ever well-formed, no topic held for even a clause.
All three measurements agree the model is operating at roughly bigram level.

CONSEQUENCE FOR THE STUDY.  We have been ranking configs by a metric that is
~95% marginal-distribution fitting, using margins (0.02-0.07) that are the
size of the noise floor.  That explains why a dozen carefully-reasoned
promotions net to approximately zero: they were mostly not measuring context
modelling at all.  Loss alone cannot drive this search.

RECOMMENDED CHANGE OF OBJECTIVE (for the user to rule on):
  * make REACH (mean excess, and the c256/c511 tail) the primary metric, with
    loss as the constraint -- it is the only measurement that separates the
    configs (e30 +0.073 at c511 vs b5 -0.068 while their losses tie);
  * report every config against the bigram floor at MATCHED token budget, so
    "better loss" cannot be claimed for something a count table also does;
  * treat capacity/context (n_hidden, kernel, context) as the likely binding
    constraint: context 16 with 3x3 kernels may simply be too small a state
    to carry document-scale information, which no amount of clock/ema/init
    tuning can fix.

============================================================================
2026-08-24 11:45  TRANSFORMER REFERENCE (exp/pythia_ref.py) + C1 = 7x7 LAUNCHED
============================================================================
Same held-out set as everything else (openwebtext train[-3000:], minlen 512,
maxlen 1024, first 400 docs, 353,934 tokens), same (x,y) pairs.

  model / method            params    body    nats     ppl
  pythia-160m               162.3M   85.1M   3.1456    23.2
  pythia-70m                 70.4M   18.9M   3.5582    35.1
  pythia-31m                 30.5M    4.7M   3.8183    45.5
  bigram, 226M-token fit        --      --   5.5830   265.9
  e30 @140K                  56.1M   30.4M   5.6617   287.6
  VLA b5 @100K               56.1M   30.4M   5.7528   315.1
  bigram, 28M-token fit         --      --   5.8279   339.6   (matched budget)
  unigram                       --      --   7.5732  1945.4

THE COMPARABLE TRANSFORMER IS 1.93 NATS BETTER -- 7x lower perplexity.

And the comparison is stacked in VLA's favour on both sides:
  * pythia was trained on the Pile, so openwebtext is CROSS-DOMAIN for it and
    IN-DOMAIN for VLA.  Its true openwebtext number is lower than 3.8183.
  * parameter counts do not mean what the names suggest.  pythia-31m is 30.5M
    TOTAL but 12.9M of that is the 50304x256 embedding table; its transformer
    BODY is 4.7M.  VLA has 30.4M trainable conv parameters on top of the same
    25.8M frozen pythia embeddings.  VLA carries ~6.5x more compute-bearing
    parameters and loses by 1.93 nats.
Even pythia-70m's body (18.9M, still smaller than VLA's 30.4M) is 2.19 nats
ahead.  There is no parameter-count reading under which VLA is competitive.

Where VLA actually sits: between a bigram fit on 28M tokens (its own data
budget) and a bigram fit on 226M.  It beats the matched-budget bigram by 0.075
and loses to the 226M bigram by 0.170.  The transformer is not in the same
regime -- 1.77 nats below even the 226M bigram.

This is the objective reference the study lacked.  Loss targets from here on
should be quoted against BOTH floors: the bigram at matched budget (what a
count table gets for free) and pythia-31m at 3.8183 (what the architecture is
competing with).  The distance to close is 1.93 nats, not 0.05.

C1 LAUNCHED (10:17): 7x17 -> 7x7 at matched weight count.  3x17 = 51 convs,
7x7 = 49; 30.36M trainable either way, so this is a SHAPE experiment, not the
capacity experiment (capacity stays last, per user).  7 DFF states of 7 convs
vs 3 states of 17: more places to hold state and a longer clock ladder, but
RF radius 7 per write instead of 17, so a single write no longer covers the
16x16 grid (that needs C-1 = 15 convs).
--clock_base moved 13.15 -> 2.36 and this is NOT a second variable: periods
are base**li (vla.py:204), so p_max = base**(L-1).  13.15 at L=7 would give
5.2M steps and the deep layers would never write in a 1024-token document.
2.36 holds p_max at 173, identical to the baseline.  Confirmed in the log:
  L1=1, L2=2, L3=6, L4=13, L5=31, L6=73, L7=173 steps
B6 terminated at step 28180 to free the GPU (repro question was already
answered at 20K: held-out deltas +0.002 / +0.035 / +0.018 at 5/10/20K).

PHASE 2 (user, recorded for the next session): freeze the model at ~30M
parameters and focus on the DATASET.  Capacity remains the last thing to
explore.

############################################################################
2026-08-24  SESSION SUMMARY -- THE DAY THE OBJECTIVE CHANGED
############################################################################
Read this section first if you are picking up the thread cold.  Today did not
produce a better model.  It produced the reference points that show the search
we were running could not have produced one, and it is the reference points,
not the promotions, that should drive what comes next.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
1. THE NOISE FLOOR (B6), MEASURED AT LAST
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
B6 = the promoted baseline rerun FROM SCRATCH at --seed 2.  --seed sets weight
init only; the data worker is unseeded, so this is TOTAL run-to-run variance,
which is the quantity that matters when ranking two configs.

  held-out delta vs the seed-1 run:   5K +0.0020   10K +0.0353   20K +0.0176
  matched-step training delta over 20 points: mean +0.0141 sd 0.0297 max 0.0671

SO: two IDENTICAL configs differ by 0.02-0.035 on held-out and by up to 0.067
instantaneously.  Every promotion margin in this study was 0.02-0.07.  We have
been ranking configs inside their own noise for weeks.
The +0.035 at 10K did NOT persist to 20K -- it was autocorrelated noise, which
is also why single mid-run readings must never be trusted (see 3e).
B6 terminated at 28180 once the question was answered.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
2. B4 (--bn_affine) REJECTED, AND THE REJECTION NOW HAS A REASON
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
  train @5/10/15/20K   B4 6.5444 6.3017 6.1685 6.0522
                       B5 6.5772 6.3531 6.2262 6.1209   (B4 ahead throughout)
  held-out @20K        B4 6.1948  vs  B5 6.2227          (+0.028 = noise)
B4 led on EVERY training checkpoint with a margin growing to 0.069, and still
lost, because 0.069 is inside the envelope section 1 just measured.  The
pre-registered rule (beat by >0.07 or reject, since affine adds ~26K params)
gave the right answer for a reason that could not be defended when it was
written.  H1, the 80K extension built to re-test it, was terminated at 23180.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
3. CORRECTION: E30 AND B5 DIFFER BY EXACTLY ONE FLAG
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
I reported "four promotions net to zero at 100K".  WRONG -- inferred from the
promotion history instead of read from the checkpoints.  Diffing saved_args:
e30 ALREADY had lr 0.03, 3x17 and --init zero.  The only difference is
bn_track (True vs False); e30 has 102 running_* buffers, b5 has none.
So e30-vs-b5 is a clean single-variable test of --no-bn_track, and the
corrected finding is narrower: ONE 20K win became a 100K tie on loss with
worse tail reach.  B5 is nonetheless PROMOTED (user decision) -- it is simpler
and removes the train/eval mismatch, which matters for the FPGA target.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
4. --init IS INERT.  DROP IT FROM THE SEARCH SPACE.
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Generating from b5@100K with --init zero vs --init relu, same seeds:
  seed 11 offset 3 matched 94/94 = 100%    seed 14 offset 3  99%
  seed 13 offset 2 matched 90/90 = 100%    seed 15 offset 3  98%
Token-for-token IDENTICAL text after a 2-3 token offset.  The recurrent state
is contractive: both initial conditions collapse onto the same trajectory
within ~3 tokens.  vla.py:1360 claims divergence "falls to 3% by 20 bytes";
the generation evidence is stronger -- it is exact reconvergence.
--init can only ever act through training dynamics, never through inference.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
5. WHERE THE MODEL ACTUALLY SITS  (the important part)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
All on the SAME held-out set (openwebtext train[-3000:], minlen 512, maxlen
1024, first 400 docs, 353,934 tokens) and the same (x,y) pairs.

  model / method            params    body    nats     ppl
  pythia-160m               162.3M   85.1M   3.1456    23.2
  pythia-70m                 70.4M   18.9M   3.5582    35.1
  pythia-31m                 30.5M    4.7M   3.8183    45.5
  BIGRAM, infinite data         --      --   5.3017   200.7   <- hard floor
  bigram, 226M-token fit        --      --   5.5830   265.9
  e30 @140K                  56.1M   30.4M   5.6617   287.6
  VLA b5 @100K               56.1M   30.4M   5.7528   315.1
  bigram, 28M fit (matched)     --      --   5.8279   339.6
  unigram                       --      --   7.5732  1945.4

pythia-31m is 1.93 nats (7x perplexity) ahead, and the comparison is stacked
in VLA's favour BOTH ways: pythia is cross-domain here (Pile-trained) and its
transformer BODY is 4.7M against VLA's 30.4M trainable convs.  VLA carries
~6.5x more compute-bearing parameters and loses by 1.93 nats.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
6. BUT THE SLOPE SAYS "SLOWLY DECREASING", NOT "FLAT"  (user's correction)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Comparing endpoints against pythia is unfair -- pythia saw ~300B tokens, VLA
25M.  Held-out ladders were scored and fitted (exp/scaling.py):

  b5 lineage  5K..100K   1.2M..25M tokens   6.6390 -> 5.7528
  e30        60K..140K  15.0M..35M tokens   5.8684 -> 5.6617

                        floor power   log-linear   pure power   best
  VLA b5 (held-out)        0.0098       0.0092       0.0098   log-linear
  VLA e30 (held-out)       0.0143       0.0142       0.0143   log-linear
  bigram                   0.0009       0.0227       0.0209   floor power (25x)

VLA slope 0.183-0.205 nats/doubling, consistent across two independent runs,
and 3.5x the bigram's 0.053 at comparable scale.  So "the model is at bigram
level" was true of one POINT and false of the TRAJECTORY: the bigram has a
hard floor at 5.3017 that VLA is on course to pass.

FUNCTIONAL FORM IS UNDECIDED, and honestly so.  VLA residuals are sign-
scattered under both forms (no systematic curvature = insufficient range).
The bigram's log-linear residuals show the classic U (+,-,-,-,-,-,+,+), the
fingerprint of a straight line fitted to something bending to an asymptote --
which is why the bigram discriminates and VLA does not.  VLA has not bent yet.
Every fit that ALLOWS a floor puts it at E=0; that is not a claim the floor is
zero, only that the data carries no information about it.

  tokens to reach pythia-31m (3.8183):
    log-linear   1.7e10 - 3.6e10        power law   1.3e11 - 1.8e11
An order of magnitude apart, and log-linear cannot be literally true (it
predicts loss -> -inf, below the entropy of the text), so it must bend
somewhere.  WHERE it bends is the whole question and we cannot yet see it.

FALSIFIABLE NEAR-TERM PREDICTION: from 5.7528 at 25M tokens at 0.19/doubling,
VLA crosses the bigram's hard floor of 5.3017 at ~120M tokens = ~480K steps
(~44 h at current throughput).  That is the run that settles "is this just a
bigram" for good, and it is affordable.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
7. GENERATED TEXT AT 100K (10 samples, --init zero, seeds 11-20)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
LEARNED: function-word rates; capitalization as a class (Trump, Sanders,
Washington, Minnesota, Sussex); well-formed numerals in plausible ranges
(1910, 1995, 265, 35); openwebtext register (allegations, director, audit).
NOT LEARNED: no sample contains a single well-formed clause.  No agreement,
no completed prepositional phrases, quotes and parens never closed.
Punctuation over-emitted (",." ".."), newlines scattered.
SEMANTICS: zero.  seed 16 runs collapse -> alcohol -> veteran -> 1910 ->
demographic -> troops -> Ban with no topic held for even a clause.
Consistent with reach: context is worth +0.09 nats of the ~1.8 the model has
captured below unigram, i.e. ~95% of the loss gain is marginal fitting.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
8. C1 = 7x7 (terminated early, at user request, step 11820)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
3x17 = 51 convs, 7x7 = 49, 30.36M trainable either way: SHAPE, not capacity.
--clock_base moved 13.15 -> 2.36 and that is NOT a second variable: periods
are base**li (vla.py:204) so p_max = base**(L-1); 13.15 at L=7 gives 5.2M
steps and the deep layers would never write.  2.36 holds p_max at 173.
Confirmed ladder: L1=1 L2=2 L3=6 L4=13 L5=31 L6=73 L7=173.
Terminated before 20K, so the 7x7-vs-3x17 LOSS question remains OPEN.
Reach at 10K (matched-step control b1_notrack@10K was still running when this
was written -- get it from log/reach_c1.log):
  c1_7x7 @10K   P=64  mean +0.0388 | P=512 mean -0.0119  (c256 +0.010,
                c320 -0.031, c511 -0.069), runaway 0.0%
References at 100K+: b5 +0.0948, e30 +0.1286, wnotap +0.0756, wexp +0.0023.
Do NOT compare the 10K number to those; only to the 10K control.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
9. WHAT THIS MEANS FOR THE SEARCH  (proposed, for the user to rule on)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Loss alone cannot drive this search: margins of 0.02-0.07 are the noise floor
(1), and the metric is ~95% marginal fitting (7).  That is why a dozen
carefully-reasoned promotions net to approximately zero.  Proposed:
  a. Quote every result against BOTH floors -- the bigram at MATCHED token
     budget (what a count table gets free) and pythia-31m at 3.8183 (what the
     architecture competes with).  The gap to close is 1.93 nats, not 0.05.
  b. Prefer experiments that move the SLOPE, not the intercept.  A config that
     is 0.03 better at 20K is noise; one that changes nats/doubling is real.
  c. Make reach primary and loss a constraint -- reach is the only measurement
     that separates configs (e30 +0.073 at c511 vs b5 -0.068 while losses tie).
  d. Run the 480K-step baseline (6) -- it discriminates log-linear from power
     law AND settles the bigram question, in one run.

PHASE 2 (user): freeze the model at ~30M params and focus on the DATASET.
Capacity (n_hidden, kernel, context) remains the LAST thing to explore.

--------------------------------------------------------------------------
10. TOOLING FIXED TODAY
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
* exp/idle_watch.sh counted only "python vla.py" as busy, so a legitimately
  running reach probe read as a dead GPU and fired a false alarm.  Now counts
  score/reach/gencheck/memtest/genstate, and reports the 4 MOST RECENT driver
  logs instead of four hardcoded ones that had gone stale on 2026-08-23.
* watch.sh processes outlived their terminated trainers twice (H1, B6) and had
  to be reaped by hand.  When you kill a run, kill its watcher in the same
  breath, or a later report will describe a run that no longer exists.
* NEW exp/ngram.py    -- bigram/unigram floor on the exact score.py held-out.
* NEW exp/pythia_ref.py -- HF transformer reference on the same held-out set.
* NEW exp/scaling.py  -- fits floor-power / log-linear / pure-power, prints
  residual sign runs, extrapolates to a target loss.  Data in scaling_data.json.
* score.py takes POSITIONAL checkpoints, not --load (cost one failed run).
* No scipy in the dev env; scaling.py grids the floor and solves the remaining
  log-linear problem in closed form instead.

############################################################################
2026-08-24 12:40  --no-bn_track REVERSED.  BASELINE RESTORED TO THE e30 CONFIG.
############################################################################
User question that exposed it: with tracked stats the BN folds into the
preceding conv2d and is free at inference -- does --no-bn_track add inference
cost?  Answer: yes, and the cost is not arithmetic, it is CORRECTNESS.

MECHANISM.  PyTorch sets bn_training = (running_mean is None) even in .eval(),
so with track_running_stats=False BatchNorm normalizes by the CURRENT BATCH at
inference.  The prediction for one document therefore depends on the other
documents beside it, and at batch 1 it degenerates to InstanceNorm over the
16x16 grid.  vla.py:923 says exactly this in a comment; nobody costed it.

MEASURED (exp/batchdep.py, same 16 held-out docs, scored together vs alone):

  b5_long.final   (--no-bn_track)        e30_3x17_120k.final  (bn_track=True)
    batch16  6.2066                        batch16  5.7284
    batch8   6.6096                        batch8   5.7284
    batch1   8.0278   (+1.8212)            batch1   5.7284   (-0.0000)
    max per-doc |b16-b1| = 2.0721          max per-doc |b16-b1| = 0.0001

At batch 1 the --no-bn_track model scores 8.0278 nats -- WORSE THAN THE
UNIGRAM FLOOR (7.5732).  Deployed one stream at a time, which is what a text
generator is and what the FPGA would be, it is worse than a frequency table.
The tracked model is batch-invariant to 1e-4, exactly as fusion requires.

CONSEQUENCES, including for numbers already reported today:
 * Every held-out score in this study was computed at batch 400 (score.py
   batches all docs).  For bn_track models that is the true single-stream
   number.  For --no-bn_track models it FLATTERS them by ~1.8 nats.
 * b5@100K "5.7528" is a batch-400 figure.  Its single-stream loss is far
   worse.  e30@140K 5.6617 is real at any batch.
 * The 10 generation samples analysed at 12:00 came from b5 at BATCH 1, i.e.
   from a model 1.8 nats worse than the number quoted beside them.  The
   "bigram-level text" reading was of a crippled configuration; e30's text
   should be re-examined before that conclusion is carried forward.
 * The b5-lineage scaling ladder is still internally consistent (all points
   batch 400), so the SLOPE stands; the LEVEL does not.  The e30 ladder is
   valid at any batch.
 * --no-bn_track cannot be fused into the preceding conv, needs a per-channel
   mean/var reduction over the whole feature map before any output can leave
   the layer, and is data-dependent -- so on FPGA it breaks streaming dataflow
   entirely.  That is the real cost, not the ~0.2% arithmetic.

BASELINE RESTORED (exp/run.sh) by dropping --no-bn_track.  This is the e30
configuration, and e30@140K (5.6617 held-out, batch-invariant) is once again
BOTH the recommended config and the best trained weights -- they had diverged
since the promotion this morning.
GENERAL LESSON, worth applying beyond this flag: every metric in this study is
computed in a batched regime the deployed model will never see.  Any option
that makes inference batch-dependent must be checked with exp/batchdep.py
BEFORE it is promoted, not after.
full-history: clean mean  6.44568  median  6.54488  runaway  0.0% of scored tokens

2026-08-24 12:26  REACH: 7x7 vs 3x17 AT MATCHED 10K STEPS
--------------------------------------------------------------------------
Both probed identically (800 docs, 500 rows, len 1024).  BOTH are --no-bn_track
models (c1 was launched at 10:17, before the reversal), so both sit in the
flattered batch-dependent regime -- but they sit in it EQUALLY and were probed
at the same batch, so the 7x7-vs-3x17 SHAPE comparison is internally valid.
Neither is a deployable model.

  P=64   mean excess     7x7 +0.0388      3x17 +0.0136     (2.9x)
    c1   +0.174 / +0.117   c2  +0.169 / +0.072   c4  +0.125 / +0.033
    c8   +0.091 / +0.022   c16 +0.077 / +0.012   c32 +0.064 / +0.007
  P=512  mean excess     7x7 -0.0119      3x17 -0.0072
    c32  +0.102 / +0.025   c64 +0.057 / +0.011   c96  +0.066 / +0.027
    c128 +0.049 / +0.018   c192 +0.043 / +0.007  c256 +0.010 / -0.041
    c320 -0.031 / -0.006   c511 -0.069 / -0.052

7x7 USES MORE CONTEXT AT EVERY RANGE OUT TO c192, by 2-5x.  The gap is widest
in the c4..c32 band, exactly where the extra DFF states (7 vs 3) and the longer
clock ladder should act.  The truncated receptive field (RF radius 7, below the
15 needed to cross a 16x16 grid in one write) did NOT cost short-range reach.
Both are NEGATIVE in the P=512 tail, and both means at P=512 are ~0: at 10K
steps neither model benefits from long context yet.  For scale, b5 reached
P=512 mean +0.0948 by 100K, so reach grows a lot with training and a 10K
number says nothing about the 100K value.

STATUS: the 7x7 LOSS question is still open (C1 terminated at 11820, short of
the 20K decision point).  Reach is the one signal we have, and it favours 7x7.
The clean re-run would be 7x7 on the RESTORED baseline (bn_track on), which
also makes it the first shape experiment measured in a deployable regime.

############################################################################
2026-08-24 14:45  7x7 REVERTED.  CUMULATIVE MOMENTUM REJECTED.  BASELINE = e30.
############################################################################
Two adoptions were made today and BOTH are now reverted.  Both were validated
in a regime the deployed model does not occupy.  That is the pattern to carry
forward, not the individual flags.

(1) --no-bn_track (promoted 09:00, reverted 12:40): eval used live batch stats,
    so batch-1 generation cost +1.82 nats and could not be fused on FPGA.
(2) 7x7 (adopted 12:45, reverted 14:20): chosen on a REACH result measured on
    --no-bn_track checkpoints, where eval-mode BatchNorm uses batch statistics
    and therefore CANNOT show a train/eval gap.  With tracking on, 7x7 has one:

      config           train@5K   held-out   gap       runaway%
      3x17  m=0.1       6.7144     6.6974   -0.017        0.0
      7x7   m=0.1       6.7889     8.4471   +1.658        2.2
      7x7   m=0.01      6.6469     8.3565   +1.710        0.5
      7x7   (e24)       6.6230       nan      --        100.0

    Training loss is indistinguishable across all four (6.62-6.79); the models
    diverge only when eval switches to running statistics.  e24_lr03 is an
    INDEPENDENT earlier 7x7+bn_track run at the same lr and is 100% runaway,
    so this is a property of the shape, not of one run.
    --bn_momentum moves the runaway rate at the margin (2.2% -> 0.5%) and does
    NOT touch the 1.66-nat gap.  n_layers is the variable, not momentum.

(3) --bn_momentum 0 (cumulative average) REJECTED at step 920, and the failure
    is instant and enormous.  Identical config, identical loss and gradient at
    step 0, dff_max at step 0:

      e25_3x17    (m=0.1)   dff_max          1.1
      b1_notrack  (m=0.1)   dff_max         52.7
      d1_7x7      (m=0.1)   dff_max         18.7
      c_cummom    (m=0)     dff_max 115,215,974,400.0

    Eleven orders of magnitude.  vla.py maps 0 -> torch momentum=None correctly
    (line 1214, `self.bn_momentum or None`), so this is real behaviour, not a
    flag bug.  LIKELY MECHANISM, stated as a hypothesis consistent with the
    data and not verified: running_var is INITIALIZED to 1.0.  With m=0.1 that
    init survives as 0.9*1.0 after the first batch and acts as a floor under
    1/sqrt(var+eps).  Cumulative averaging discards it immediately -- after one
    batch running_var IS the batch variance -- and early training has
    near-dead, tiny-variance channels, which then get amplified without bound.
    This is exactly the failure --bn_eps exists to damp, so `--bn_momentum 0
    --bn_eps 1e-3` is the obvious retry if cumulative averaging is ever wanted.
    The run was draining (dff_max halving every ~200 steps via --ema 0.5) and
    would have needed ~7K steps to return to O(1).

CURRENT BASELINE (exp/run.sh) = the e30 configuration, unchanged since it was
first reached, and now the only configuration measured today that is healthy on
every axis: zero train/eval gap, zero runaway, batch-invariant inference to
1e-4, grammatical generated text, best held-out on record.

############################################################################
2026-08-24  PHASE 2 OPENS.  PILE-VAL REFERENCE LADDER MEASURED.
############################################################################
Phase 2 varies ONLY the data; the model is frozen at the e30 baseline.  The
first corpus is the Pile, streamed.  Before any pile RESULT can be read, the
pile ladder had to exist -- PLAN.txt's floors were all web numbers and a corpus
has its own information floor.  A pile score read against web's 5.3017 would
have been misjudged by half a nat.

TOOLING (all committed, all measured on IDENTICAL documents)
  Document selection moved into one shared heldout_docs() in exp/memtest.py,
  imported by exp/score.py, exp/pythia_ref.py and exp/ngram.py.  Each of the
  three used to select held-out docs with its own inline loop; the moment a
  second corpus existed, that duplication was a way to compare two models on
  two different held-out sets and never notice.  All three now take --corpus
  {tiny,web,pile}.  exp/score.py writes exp/score.<corpus>.json instead of one
  flat exp/score.json, which used to let a pile score overwrite a web score
  under the same checkpoint key.
  VERIFICATION that the refactor did not drift: pythia-31m on web rescored
  3.8183, reproducing the reference table value above EXACTLY, and the pile
  held-out came back at 358,899 tokens from both exp/pythia_ref.py and
  exp/ngram.py.  Same documents, independently reached.

PILE-VAL LADDER (400 docs, minlen 512, maxlen 1024, 358,899 tokens)
  fit for the n-grams: 20,000 streamed docs of the_pile_deduplicated train,
  28.6M tokens.  Learning curve, per-document means:

    fit tokens   coverage   unigram   bigram   bigram median
      3.6M         64.8%     8.0547   7.0277      6.8284
      7.1M         71.9%     7.9988   6.7787      6.5965
     14.3M         78.1%     7.9609   6.5295      6.3910
     28.6M         82.9%     7.9470   6.3511      6.2352

  Unigram has converged (8.066 -> 7.956 over 8x data).  Bigram has NOT: deltas
  per doubling 0.249, 0.249, 0.178, coverage still climbing.  See the caveats
  in the handoff block before quoting an asymptote.

PYTHIA-31m CHARACTERIZED (the comparison target; note there is no pythia-30m,
the suite goes 14m/31m/70m/160m)
    gpt_neox, 6 layers, hidden 256, 8 heads, 2048 positions, vocab 50304
    params  30.49M TOTAL = 25.76M embedding (84.5%, untied) + 4.74M BODY
    pile-val  3.2315 clean / 3.5037 median / 0.0% runaway
    web       3.8183 clean / 3.8117 median / 0.0% runaway
  84.5% of "31M" is a lookup table.  Its compute-bearing body is 4.74M against
  VLA's 30.36M trainable convolutions -- VLA carries ~6.4x more compute AND
  consumes pythia's 25.76M embeddings for free.  Both facts flatter VLA; the
  parameter-count framing understates how far ahead the transformer is.

CORPUS SANITY CHECK -- the Pile is NOT topic-partitioned by shard
  Asked because dolma3's hub shards ARE (see vla.py's _LOCAL_DIRS note): its
  first shard is one topic, so streaming trains on one topic at a time.  Three
  checks, all negative for the Pile:
   1. 300 docs from the head of shards 0/412/825/1237/1649 of 1650: near
      identical mix in every shard (web prose 73-82%, code 8-11%, 6-8 distinct
      categories each).  Marker-based classifier, since the deduplicated
      re-release dropped the `meta` column -- valid for COMPARING shards, not
      as ground truth for the Pile's real subset weights.
   2. The test that actually matters, since shard-level uniformity would not
      catch topic BLOCKS inside a shard: run length of consecutive same-category
      documents over 2000 docs in stream order = 3.41 mean / 39 max, against
      3.50 / 26 for the same labels randomly permuted.  Indistinguishable, i.e.
      globally shuffled.  Longest non-dominant-class run: 10 documents.
   3. vla.py's streaming path calls .shuffle(buffer_size=10000, seed=...) on a
      1650-shard IterableDataset, which reorders the SHARDS as well as
      buffering -- confirmed, three seeds start in three different regions of
      the corpus, none matching raw order.
  One real but harmless gradient: stackexchange rises 2.7% (shard 0) to 10%
  (shard 1649).  Every category is present in every shard and shard-order
  shuffling neutralizes position, so it is a gradient, not a partition.
  NOT CHECKED: pile_nc is a different repo (monology/pile-uncopyrighted, jsonl
  .zst layout).  Re-run this check before trusting it.

MONITORING CADENCE CHANGED (vla.py, user request)
  --gen_interval N   free-running GEN sample every N steps (0 = --checkpoint,
                     so every pre-existing invocation is unchanged)
  --eval_interval N  held-out eval every N steps, reported as clean/median/
                     runaway% the way exp/score.py does
  --eval_docs N      documents in that eval set (default 400, matching above)
  exp/run.sh now emits --gen_interval 500 --eval_interval 5000.  These are
  observation cadence, not model configuration: no weight, optimizer state or
  data-stream change, so runs before and after compare directly.  The eval set
  is built ONCE and reused, so successive EVAL lines differ only by the weights.
  Cost 137s per eval, ~8.4% of a 5000-step interval.  Step 0 is skipped: it
  scores the untrained model, which is ln(vocab) by construction (measured
  10.8221 against a 10.8258 ceiling) and is not worth 137s at every run start.

  BUG, MINE, AND THE PHASE 1 LESSON REPEATED.  The first --eval_interval run
  CRASHED at step 5000, on the first training step after the first eval:
    RuntimeError: The size of tensor a (400) must match ... b (250)
  The eval scores at 400 rows, training runs at 250.  _init_dff() rebuilds
  self.dff AND self.clk together on a batch-width change; the eval restored
  only dff, so _init_dff saw a matching 250-wide dff, skipped the rebuild, and
  the next training step ran a 250-row batch against a 400-row clock.  vla.py
  states the contract that was violated: "A clock belongs to a context, not to
  the model."  generate() obeys it (local state) which is why GEN was fine.
  Fixed by restoring dff and clk together, in a `finally` -- the outer except
  exists so an eval failure cannot kill a run, but a fault raised MID-scoring
  would have skipped the restore and caused this same crash THROUGH the guard.
  Verified by fault injection: eval throws, warns, disables itself, training
  continues.
  THE REAL FAILURE WAS THE SMOKE TEST.  It used --n_layers 1 with no --ema, so
  clocked=False and self.clk was permanently None -- the bug was structurally
  invisible.  That is METHOD RULE 2 ("measure in the regime you will deploy
  in"), the lesson Phase 1 calls its most expensive, repeated verbatim.  Any
  smoke test of the training loop MUST use --clock fixed --ema 0.5 --accumulate
  and a batch width different from --eval_docs.

SEED POLICY CHANGED (user request)
  exp/run.sh no longer passes --seed 1; each run draws from os.urandom, prints
  it, and stores it in saved_args.  Rationale: a fixed seed never bought
  reproducibility here.  --seed pins weight init, the HF shuffle and generation
  sampling, but NOT the data -- 12 builder threads race for the iterator lock,
  so batch composition is thread-scheduling dependent, which is why two runs of
  the same config at the same seed differ by 0.02-0.035.  What --seed 1 really
  did was make every experiment share ONE draw from the init distribution.
  Pass --seed <N> explicitly to PAIR against an older run.  exp/report.sh now
  prints the seed and dataset on every row, since neither is constant any more.
