############################################################################
2026-08-24  COMPUTE BUDGET, AND FOUR WAYS TO BUY TRAINING THROUGHPUT
############################################################################
Prompted by a matched-budget run: pythia-31m trained from scratch on the SAME
pile stream (pythia.py, frozen pretrained embeddings, 1B tokens) reached ~3.94
median on pile-val in ~2.5 HOURS at 110K tokens/s, while VLA needs ~16 days to
see the same 1B tokens at 765 tokens/s.  That is a 140x wall-clock gap and it
is worth understanding before it is either panicked about or dismissed.

WHY VLA IS SLOW.  It is not poor parallelisation.  It is arithmetic.
  51 convs of 3x3x256x256 = 30.08M weights, each applied at all 16x16 = 256
  spatial positions, so FLOPs/token = 2 * 30.08M * 256 = 15.54 GFLOP.
  A transformer costs ~2*params: pythia-31m's 4.74M body is 0.0158 GFLOP/token
  at ctx 512.  VLA does ~1000x more arithmetic per token.
  This is the CLAUDE.md design working as intended -- a small weight ROM driving
  a very large state -- but it means a GPU tokens/s benchmark measures precisely
  the axis the architecture trades AWAY.

  FLOPs/token (forward)          state @ batch 1        FLOP/byte of weight
    VLA 3x17    15.54 G (const)    384 KB (const)              256
    pythia-31m   0.016 G @512     3.0 MB @512                    1
                 0.110 G @8192     192 MB @32768
  VLA is COMPUTE-bound with almost no memory traffic; the transformer is
  MEMORY-bound.  A GPU has the bandwidth to hide that, which is why the GPU
  comparison flatters the transformer.

  HONEST LIMIT OF THE "NO KV CACHE" ARGUMENT: it is a MEMORY win, not a FLOPs
  win.  Attention alone does not cost what VLA costs until ~1.26M tokens of
  context.  The claim to make is constant state / constant latency / tiny
  weight bandwidth -- never "fewer operations".

  DEPLOYMENT TARGET (user, 2026-08-24): inference is a SINGLE CUSTOM CHIP WITH
  NO EXTERNAL COMPONENTS.  Everything lives in on-die SRAM.  That converts the
  memory column above from a performance argument into a FEASIBILITY one: a KV
  cache reaching 192 MB at 32K context cannot be put on-die at any context the
  product needs, while a 384 KB state that never grows can.  Arithmetic is the
  cheap, predictable resource on fixed silicon; bandwidth is the scarce one.
  So VLA is trading the resource the chip has for the resource it does not.

FOUR WAYS TO BUY TRAINING THROUGHPUT.  PROPOSALS, NOT RESULTS -- none has been
run.  Ordered cheapest-and-safest first.  (1)-(3) change the model and need
real experiments; (4) and the DDP note change nothing about it.

  1. MAKE --clock GATE COMPUTE, NOT JUST WRITES.   2.77x, no capacity lost.
     Today _layers() runs every conv every step and the clock only decides
     whether the RESULT IS STORED -- vla.py says so outright: "a layer that does
     not write this step keeps the state it had, while still passing the output
     it just computed up the stack."  So the 1 / 13 / 173 ladder buys longer
     memory at ZERO compute saving.  If a layer reused its cached output between
     ticks instead of recomputing, compute drops to (1 + 1/13 + 1/173)/3 = 0.361
     of current: 15.54 -> 5.61 GFLOP/token.
     This is arguably what the clock was always for.  It DOES change the model
     -- deep-layer outputs go stale between ticks, and the decoder reads the top
     layer -- so it is a real experiment, not a refactor.  Largest structural
     win available, and it REMOVES work rather than adding parameters (rule 7).

  2. DEPTHWISE-SEPARABLE 3x3.   8.7x fewer MACs and params.
     Per conv at C=256: dense 9*256*256 = 589,824 MACs; separable 9*256 + 256*256
     = 67,840.  Maps onto the same dataflow IE120R already uses.  Costs capacity,
     so it would need widening to compensate -- which is why it is Phase 3 and
     not now: Phase 2 froze the model deliberately.

  3. REDUCED SPATIAL RESOLUTION MID-STACK.   ~4x on the layers it touches.
     All 51 convs run at full 16x16, but after two or three 3x3s the receptive
     field already spans the grid, so the full-resolution depth may be buying
     nothing.  A 16->8->16 middle section is the cheapest version to test.

  4. bf16 AUTOCAST ON THE TRAINER.   ~2x, ZERO architecture change.
     vla.py trains in fp32.  This does not make the model better, it makes us
     learn about it twice as fast -- which against a 16-day run is the highest
     leverage item here.  torch.autocast keeps BatchNorm in fp32 automatically,
     so the bn_track behaviour Phase 1 fought for is preserved.  Verify with
     exp/batchdep.py and a matched-seed loss curve before adopting.

  AND SCALE-OUT, which is not on this list because it is not a model change:
  --batch is N INDEPENDENT document streams with a one-token gradient horizon.
  No sequence sharding, no pipeline bubbles -- DDP scales near-linearly on
  gradient all-reduce alone.  The one real decision is BatchNorm across
  replicas: per-replica BN is probably both correct and cheaper here, but it is
  a decision, not a default, and PLAN.txt's history says BN details decide runs.

NOT YET MEASURED, AND IT IS THE NUMBER THE PRODUCT CLAIM RESTS ON: batch-1
inference. There is no benchmark of VLA vs a transformer on tokens/s at batch 1,
state bytes, and weight bandwidth across context lengths.  Everything above is
arithmetic, not measurement.  exp/infer_bench.py is the missing tool.

p2_pile_4m ran 11.4% of its 4M-step target and was stopped intact at user
request to free the GPU for the synthetic pre-pretraining experiment.  Nothing
failed: zero restarts, zero HALTs, 2 days of continuous training.

  RESUME IT WITH (it will pick up from the checkpoint and draw a fresh seed):
    ./exp/launch.sh exp/longrun.sh p2_pile_4m 4000000 20 --dataset pile \
        --streaming --eval_interval 20000
  Weights preserved: checkpoint/p2_pile_4m.stopped_456840.pt, plus 100K
  milestone stashes at 100000/200000/300000/400000.

WHAT IT ESTABLISHED (pile-val median, vs pythia-31m from scratch on the SAME
stream at matched tokens -- see pythia.py):

    tokens     VLA     pythia    delta
       5M    6.5494    6.9124    VLA -0.363
      10M    6.2008    6.6510    VLA -0.450     <- VLA crossed the pile bigram
      20M    5.9966    5.7764        +0.220        floor (6.2352) here
      60M    5.7664    4.6842        +1.082
     100M    5.5526    4.4103        +1.142

  VLA leads at 5M and 10M, is overtaken between 10M and 15M, and the gap
  stabilises near 1.14 nats.  Slopes per doubling, nested windows to 100M:

      5M->100M   VLA -0.231   pythia -0.579
     20M->100M   VLA -0.191   pythia -0.588
     50M->100M   VLA -0.242   pythia -0.400
     80M->100M   VLA -0.320   pythia -0.255   <- VLA steeper for the first time

  VLA HAS NOT BENT.  Its slope sits in a -0.19..-0.32 band across every window
  with no monotone decay, while pythia's decayed from -0.588 to -0.255.  A
  flattening was flagged at 60M and RETRACTED at 65M -- it was one high point,
  and the nested windows that seemed to confirm it all ended on that same
  point.  Treat sub-0.11 interval moves as noise; that is the measured floor.

REACH, and a negative result worth having.  Probed on pile, --len 1024:

                       full-history   P=64      P=512    c511
    p2 @  25M tokens      5.9956     +0.5096   +0.3612  +0.236
    p2 @ 100M tokens      5.4569     +0.5955   +0.3495  +0.227
    e30 @ 35M (web!)      5.6731     +0.2107   +0.1286  +0.073

  Between 25M and 100M tokens loss improved 0.54 nats and REACH DID NOT MOVE
  (P=512 excess 0.3612 -> 0.3495, c511 0.236 -> 0.227, both slightly DOWN).
  So the 2.8x larger excess against e30 is the CORPUS and the probe settings,
  not the model learning longer dependencies.  The context capability is
  present early and is not bought with data; the loss gains from 25M->100M are
  better local prediction.  This makes the 7x7 reach question (see OPEN) more
  interesting, not less: reach looks architectural.
  The model does use context -- c0 +2.30 decaying smoothly to c511 +0.227,
  unsaturated at 511 tokens on a 16x16 state with a one-token gradient horizon.

  TOOL TRAP, cost an hour: exp/reach.py --periods 512 with the default --len 384
  returns EXACTLY +0.0000 at every offset.  A period longer than the row cannot
  splice, so each "spliced" row is one document and equals full history by
  construction.  A zero reach is what a BROKEN PROBE and a CONTEXT-BLIND MODEL
  look like alike.  Always pass --len >= max(--periods).

================================================================================
                 D1  d1_dict_10ep  -- THE DICTIONARY MEMORIZATION PROBE
                            opened 2026-08-28
================================================================================
A DIFFERENT KIND OF EXPERIMENT.  Every corpus in Phase 2 so far is effectively
infinite at our budget: the model sees each document once and the question is
how well it GENERALIZES.  This one is the opposite.  MongoDB/english-words-
definitions is a CLOSED corpus of 466,670 dictionary entries, ~20M tokens, and
it is meant to be repeated until it is learned.  The question is not "how low
does held-out go" -- there IS no held-out here, deliberately -- it is:

    CAN THIS ARCHITECTURE MEMORIZE, and does a recurrent CNN behave differently
    when the data is LIMITED rather than effectively infinite?

That second question is the user's, and it is the reason this is worth GPU time
even though a memorized dictionary is not itself a useful artifact.  Everything
measured in Phase 1 and Phase 2 was measured in the infinite-data regime, where
a 30M-parameter model never revisits a document.  Nothing in PLAN.txt says what
happens when it revisits one twenty times.

WHAT SUCCESS LOOKS LIKE.  Train loss is the ONLY scalar, so it carries the whole
signal -- and by itself it is weak evidence, because loss falls on any corpus.
The discriminating observations are:
  1. A STEP DOWN AT AN EPOCH BOUNDARY.  The log now emits an EPOCH line (see
     below) when the corpus wraps.  Generalization produces a smooth curve
     through those points; MEMORIZATION produces a visible drop just after one,
     because the model is now being re-asked questions it has already been told
     the answers to.  This is the cleanest single discriminator available.
  2. THE GENERATION SAMPLES.  The periodic GEN block now samples a LIST of
     prompts (--gen_prompts), and the NULL prompt is always first, so the
     free-running sample every earlier run produced is kept and the keyed probes
     are additional.  Four lines per interval:
       [<STX>]      free-running: what the model writes unprompted
       [aardvark]   in corpus, 2 senses -- direct term->definition recall
       [telescope]  in corpus, 3 senses -- does sense COUNT survive
       [zorbulate]  NOT IN THE CORPUS -- the confabulation control
     zorbulate is the load-bearing one.  A model that has learned the FORM will
     emit a fluent, confident, entirely invented definition for it.  Note the
     corpus itself contains rejection entries ("Pastromis is not a recognized
     word and may be a misspelling of 'pastrami'"), so a genuinely well-fit model
     has the option of REFUSING zorbulate -- and doing so would be much stronger
     evidence than any single correct definition.
     Ground truth for aardvark, from row 284 of the corpus:
       aardvark
       1. An aardvark is a nocturnal, insectivorous mammal native to Africa,
          with a long snout and a sticky tongue used for catching ants and
          termites.
       2. The aardvark is the only living species in the order Tubulidentata.
     A model that has memorized emits that.  A model that has learned the FORM
     but not the CONTENT emits a fluent, confident, wrong definition in exactly
     that shape -- which is the interesting failure and the one to watch for.
     Do not score this by eye alone; a proper probe over N random headwords with
     an overlap metric is the missing tool (exp/dictprobe.py, not yet written --
     every 5000-step checkpoint is stashed, so it can be applied retroactively).

CONFIG.  Frozen baseline, data-only change, per the Phase 2 rule:
    ./exp/launch.sh exp/longrun.sh d1_dict_10ep 800000 20 \
        --dataset wdef --eval_interval 0 \
        --gen_prompts aardvark,telescope,zorbulate
  800,000 steps = 200M tokens ~ 10 epochs ~ 3.0 days at 3.05 steps/s.
  --eval_interval 0: there is no held-out split and inventing one by holding out
  dictionary entries would measure generalization to UNSEEN WORDS, which is a
  different (also interesting, also later) experiment.  Train loss it is.
  NOT --streaming: the exact-shuffle path below requires a materialized Dataset.

CORPUS FACTS (measured, pythia31m tokenizer, 4000-row sample):
  466,670 rows          1.72 senses/entry (median 2, max 6)
  ~0.05% of rows have an empty definition list and are skipped
  --wdef_format term  43.1 tokens/entry mean -> 20.1M tokens = 80,423 steps/epoch
  --wdef_format defs  36.6 tokens/entry mean -> 17.1M tokens = 68,278 steps/epoch
  on disk 80.1 MiB -- three orders of magnitude below any other corpus here

WHAT WAS ADDED TO vla.py FOR THIS (all four are new, 2026-08-28):
  'wdef' in _TEXT_DATASETS + _fmt_wdef.  The payload is 'definitions', a LIST of
    strings; there is no 'text' column.  _fmt_wdef renders the headword then its
    numbered senses.
  --gen_prompts.  The periodic GEN block sampled ONE prompt; it now samples a
    list, with the null prompt always first and unconditional so no run loses its
    free-running sample.  generate() runs on a local dff/clock, so extra samples
    cannot perturb training state.
  epoch on the TEXT line.  'TEXT step N examples E tokens T epoch wdef=3'.  On an
    infinite corpus this is noise; on a closed one it is the experiment's clock.
  --wdef_format term|defs.  'term' (default) keeps the headword, which is what
    makes the memorization question answerable from a sample -- a term->definition
    MAPPING can be probed. 'defs' emits the gloss sentences alone: still a
    memorization target, but with no key to query it by, so recall cannot be
    distinguished from fluent paraphrase.  The default is a judgment call and it
    is recorded here because it CHANGES WHAT THE EXPERIMENT MEASURES.
  EPOCH lines.  _next_example already looped and reshuffled on exhaustion
    (seed + epoch); it just never said so.  It now writes an EPOCH line to
    log/exp.$M, interleaved with the STEP lines, because for a repeated corpus
    the wrap point is an experimental landmark and not bookkeeping.
  Exact-permutation shuffle for small corpora (_EXACT_SHUFFLE_BYTES, 1 GiB).
    _make_iter deliberately avoids Dataset.shuffle() -- on 45.7GB fineweb-edu it
    caused 445x read amplification and zero steps in nine minutes.  That is a
    property of random access into a huge arrow file, not of shuffle(); an 80 MiB
    corpus sits in page cache.  It MATTERS here: under the 10k-row buffer shuffle
    an entry's neighbours are largely the same on every pass, so each epoch would
    re-present the same local orderings and a "memorization" result could be an
    artifact of that correlation.  A full permutation makes each epoch an
    independent ordering.

STARTUP TRANSIENT -- MEASURED, wdef-SPECIFIC, AND IT SELF-CORRECTS.  Checked before
launch so it is not mistaken for a fault later.  CPU, real batch 250, monitor 4,
against a TINY control run at identical settings:

    step        0        4        8       12       16       20
    wdef    0.062   12.528    4.261    0.257    0.093    0.104   dff_std
    tiny    0.015    0.026    0.051      --       --       --    dff_std

Step 0 matches the healthy baseline (c1 opened at dff_std 0.147, grad 645; wdef at
0.062, grad 704).  Then wdef spikes to dff_std 12.5 at step 4 and is back to 0.26
by step 12, settling at 0.09-0.10 -- which is exactly where c1 sat at step 20
(0.099).  The tiny control at the SAME batch does NOT spike, so this is a property
of THIS CORPUS, not of early training in general.  Most likely cause: 43-token
documents mean many of the 250 slots hit <ETX> and reset within the first few
steps (the examples counter runs 2 -> 13 -> 31 over steps 0-8), while the
BatchNorm running stats are still near their init.  It resolves itself and is not
a blocker.  WHAT WOULD BE NEW: dff_std still above ~1 at step 100+.  If that
happens the run is not converging and --clip / --state_norm are the levers.

CAVEAT ON THE 3 SHORT-DOCUMENT CONSEQUENCES.  Entries are ~43 tokens, an order of
magnitude shorter than any corpus used here before.  That means (a) <ETX> and a
context reset arrive constantly, so this trains the model's DOCUMENT-START
behaviour far harder than its long-range carry, (b) --preroll reaches its first
<ETX> in 13 steps rather than 22+, and (c) reach/context-usage numbers from this
run are NOT comparable to any other run and should not be put in the same table.

================================================================================
              B1  b1_bvocab_400k  -- MEMORIZATION AT 732x LESS DATA
                            prepared 2026-08-29
================================================================================
THE HYPOTHESIS (the user's, and it is the reason for the run): a CNN is very
parameter-efficient but needs MANY PASSES over the data to memorize, where a
large transformer may memorize in far fewer.  d1_dict_10ep cannot test that --
at 78,667 steps per epoch it gets ~10 passes in three days.  Shrinking the
corpus by 732x buys ~3,700 passes in a day and a half, which is the regime where
"how many epochs to memorize" is actually measurable.

THE CORPUS.  basic_dict.txt, 848 documents, AUTHORED not downloaded:
    <word>: <sentence 1> <sentence 2> <sentence 3>
one per line, e.g.
    apple: I picked an apple from the tree and ate it on the walk home. She
    sliced three apples into the bowl for the pie. The apple tree behind the
    barn blooms every spring.
  848 docs   31.7 tokens/doc (median 31, min 25, max 44)
  26,875 tokens/epoch  =  107.5 steps/epoch at batch 250
    200k steps = 1,860 epochs = 18.3 h
    400k steps = 3,721 epochs = 36.5 h     <- the planned run
    800k steps = 7,442 epochs = 73.1 h
  Compare d1/wdef: 466,357 docs, 19.67M tokens/epoch.  This is 732x smaller.

THE WORD LIST.  basic_vocab.txt, 848 words, grade 1-5, scraped from the Reading
Rockets "Basic Spelling Vocabulary List" (readingrockets.org).  Five HTML tables,
one per grade: 94 / 295 / 316 / 107 / 37.  The list is cumulative-by-introduction,
which is why the later grades are small.  Asterisks in the source mark "words
children have difficulty spelling" and were stripped.  One duplicate across
grades ('here') was dropped; grade order is preserved, so the file runs easy to
hard.  Pure ASCII, one word per line.

WHY AUTHORED SENTENCES AND NOT DICTIONARY DEFINITIONS.  The obvious build was to
filter wdef (MongoDB/english-words-definitions) down to these 848 words.  All 848
are present, but ONLY 581 match with exact case; the other 267 exist in the
corpus solely as capitalized or ALLCAPS forms, and those are overwhelmingly
ACRONYMS, not the basic word:
    be  -> BE  "the chemical symbol for Beryllium"
    cat -> CAT "an acronym for Computerized Axial Tomography"
    but -> BUT "an acronym for Bulgarian University of Technology"
    car -> CAR "Chimeric Antigen Receptor"
    did -> DID "Dissociative Identity Disorder"
    for -> FOR "a control flow statement in many programming languages"
71 of the 848 chosen definitions match an acronym/abbreviation pattern outright.
A case-insensitive filter would therefore have produced a "grade 1-5 dictionary"
in which a third of the entries define unrelated technical acronyms -- a corpus
that is still memorizable, but that no one reading the generation samples could
interpret.  Authoring the sentences sidesteps the case collision entirely and
gives full control over difficulty and length.

GENERATION PROVENANCE, stated because it matters for reading the results: the
2,544 sentences were written by eight Sonnet subagents, ~106 words each, then
validated mechanically -- 848 lines, order matched against basic_vocab.txt
line-for-line, ASCII-only, three sentences per line, root present in the body.
2,534 of 2,552 sentences are unique; 18 collided across chunk boundaries (0.7%),
which was left alone.  The file is checked in, so the exact bytes trained on are
recoverable from git rather than being a re-generation of something similar.

VLA.PY SUPPORT ADDED.  --dataset bdict, backed by --bdict_file (default
basic_dict.txt).  It is the first LOCAL corpus here: _init_one_dataset intercepts
the name and calls load_dataset('text', ...), whose default sample_by='line'
gives exactly one document per line, so shuffle, epoch wrap and _corpus_text are
identical to a hub corpus and nothing downstream knows the difference.  At 27 KB
it takes the exact-permutation shuffle branch, so every one of the ~3,700 epochs
is an independent ordering.  NOTE: blank lines become EMPTY ROWS (verified), so
basic_dict.txt must contain none -- it does not.

THE RUN (not yet launched; d1_dict_10ep still holds the GPU):
    ./exp/launch.sh exp/longrun.sh b1_bvocab_400k 400000 20 \
        --dataset bdict --eval_interval 0 \
        --gen_prompts apple:,parent:,zorbulate:
  Probes: the null prompt, two in-corpus headwords, and 'zorbulate:' which is NOT
  in the 848 -- the same confabulation control d1 uses.  With 107 steps/epoch the
  EPOCH line fires every ~35 s, so the log carries ~3,700 of them; that is the
  intended clock and the x-axis for "epochs to memorize".

WHAT TO WATCH.  Train loss is again the only scalar.  A corpus of 26,875 tokens
against 30.36M trainable parameters is ~1,130 parameters per token, so the model
CAN trivially fit it -- the question is only how many passes it takes, and
whether the loss goes to near zero or stalls at a floor.  A stall would be the
more interesting result: it would say the one-token gradient horizon, not
capacity, is what limits memorization.

================================================================================
        PP1  pp1_bvocab_e10s  -- PRE-PRETRAINING ON SIMPLE ENGLISH
                   PREPARED 2026-08-29, NOT STARTED (awaiting review)
================================================================================
THE IDEA.  Every run so far starts from --init zero on whatever corpus it is
studying.  This one is a WARM START to be consumed by a later run: train on a
small corpus of clean, simple, single-clause English until it is memorized, then
use those weights to initialize the real thing (the c1-style 85%-AI mix) and see
whether the downstream run gets anywhere better or faster.  It is cheap -- the
corpus is 94K tokens -- so the question is only whether a curriculum helps at
all, not whether this particular corpus is the right one.

THE CORPUS.  basic_dict_e10.txt + --bdict_split.
  basic_dict_e10.txt is a STRICT SUPERSET of basic_dict_e3.txt: same 848 grade
  1-5 vocabulary words, same first three sentences BYTE FOR BYTE, plus seven
  more per word.  8,480 sentences total, verified: 848 lines, 10 sentences each,
  zero superset violations, zero non-ASCII.  Keeping the prefix exact is what
  makes b1 (e3) and any e10 run comparable -- the extra text is strictly added,
  nothing was reworded.
  --bdict_split then strips the leading "word: " key and makes EVERY SENTENCE a
  document.  That is the whole point: WITH the key the corpus is a lookup table
  and the model learns "after a colon, recite"; WITHOUT it the corpus is just
  simple English, and what can transfer downstream is ordinary sentence
  structure.  b1 (keyed) and pp1 (unkeyed) are therefore different experiments
  on the same text, which is worth keeping straight.

  measured, pythia31m:
    e3  unsplit    848 docs   31.7 tok/doc   26,875 tok/epoch    108 steps/epoch
    e10 unsplit    848 docs  103.5 tok/doc   87,759 tok/epoch    351 steps/epoch
    e10 SPLIT    8,480 docs   11.0 tok/doc   93,658 tok/epoch    375 steps/epoch
                            (median 11, min 4, max 21)
  400,000 steps = 1,068 epochs = ~37 h at 3.03 steps/s.

THE RUN (do not launch without review; b1 currently holds the GPU):
    ./exp/launch.sh exp/longrun.sh pp1_bvocab_e10s 400000 20 \
        --dataset bdict --bdict_file basic_dict_e10.txt --bdict_split \
        --eval_interval 0
  NO --gen_prompts: the null prompt is sampled unconditionally, so GEN is pure
  free-running text.  That is the measurement here -- the success criterion is
  that free-running generation eventually emits VERBATIM sentences from the
  training set, which is memorization visible without any keyed probe.

  WATCH FOR: 11-token documents mean <ETX> and a context reset roughly every 11
  steps, an order of magnitude more often than any web corpus.  This trains
  document-START behaviour extremely hard, and it is the reason the resulting
  weights might transfer BADLY as well as well -- a model tuned to reset every
  11 tokens has to unlearn that on a corpus of 1000-token documents.  That risk
  is the experiment.

VLA.PY CHANGES MADE FOR THIS (2026-08-29):
  --bdict_split  as above.  Splitting is done at LOAD time, building the Dataset
    in memory, because one row becomes many documents and the line-oriented
    'text' builder cannot express that.  The epoch counter then counts passes
    over SENTENCES, which is the unit the run is measured in.
  _split_sentences  is fussier than it looks and two bugs were found by counting:
    (1) the abbreviation HEADWORDS this corpus defines ('Mr.', 'Mrs.', 'Ms.')
        must be masked or "Mr. Johnson teaches math." counts as two;
    (2) a boundary is [.!?] OPTIONALLY FOLLOWED BY A CLOSING QUOTE.  Requiring
        bare [.!?] runs `with "Dear friend." His grandmother...` together (one
        line of e3, 'dear', gave 2543 instead of 2544); allowing ANY quote
        instead splits `"No candy before dinner," Mom reminded him` at the comma.
    A fragment starting lower-case is a dialogue tag and is merged back.
    Verified: e3 -> exactly 2,544, e10 -> exactly 8,480.
  epoch IS NOW A COLUMN on every STEP line ('... examples N epoch N') instead of
    standalone EPOCH lines in the log.  chart.py matches fields by name and
    defaults missing ones to 0.0, so old logs and logs that span this change both
    parse.  The standalone line still goes to stdout (log/exp.$M.out) because it
    carries the reshuffle seed, which the column cannot.

================================================================================
    K1  k1_know80_100m  -- CURRICULUM STAGE 2: LANGUAGE + GENERAL KNOWLEDGE
                   QUEUED 2026-08-29, NOT STARTED (pp1 holds the GPU)
================================================================================
THE IDEA.  Build the training distribution UP GRADUALLY instead of dropping the
model into the final mix cold.  Stage 1 (pp1) is 8,480 single simple sentences.
Stage 2 is this: still simple, but real language and real general knowledge,
with math and code DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED so they can be introduced as stage 3.
The comparison this is eventually judged against is c1_sense_1b, which started
from --init zero on a mix that already contained math and reasoning traces.

THE MIX -- 80% AI / 20% HUMAN, no math, no code:
    AI     cosmo   0.30   Mixtral-8x7B-Instruct, cosmopedia/stories,
                          audience=young_children -- simple narrative language
           fpfaq   0.30   SmolLM2-1.7B-Instruct, finephrase/faq -- explanatory
                          Q&A over fineweb-edu documents; general knowledge
           fptut   0.20   SmolLM2-1.7B-Instruct, finephrase/tutorial -- how-to
                          and popular-science prose.  NEWLY REGISTERED for this
                          run (see below)
    HUMAN  swiki   0.12   Simple English Wikipedia -- human, general knowledge,
                          deliberately simple register
           fwedu   0.08   fineweb-edu -- human CommonCrawl pages, classifier-
                          selected for educational value
  Provenance was VERIFIED from the dataset cards on disk, not assumed: cosmopedia
  states "generated by Mixtral-8x7B-Instruct-v0.1"; finephrase is tagged
  synthetic / machine-generated with SmolLM2-1.7B-Instruct over
  fineweb-edu/sample-350BT; fineweb-edu is human web text where only the
  SELECTION was machine-driven.

WHAT WAS EXCLUDED AND WHY.
  fpmath, brt, s1k   math and reasoning traces -- the whole point of the stage
                     is that math arrives LATER.  brt's sources are
                     GeneralThought-430K / OpenR1-Math-220k / OpenThoughts-114k,
                     which is math-heavy even where it is not labelled as such.
  pile, pile_nc      contain GitHub code.
  tiny               TinyStories was in the first draft of this mix and was
                     REJECTED by the user: its deliberately restricted
                     vocabulary is LOW ENTROPY, and after pp1 (which is already
                     simple single sentences) it would be a step backwards, not
                     a widening.  This is the right instinct -- the stage is
                     supposed to ADD distribution, not re-narrow it.
  Trace amounts of arithmetic survive in any web corpus; "no math" here means no
  DEDICATED math corpus, not a guarantee of zero equations.

NEW CORPUS REGISTERED: 'fptut' = finephrase [tutorial].  Excluding math and code
left only two AI sources, which would have put 40% of the run on a single
generator config.  Only ONE shard is on disk (67,000 rows, ~27M tokens), enough
for about one pass at weight 0.20 over 100M tokens; it is in _HUB_SUBSET (so it
loads from the local snapshot, not by resolving finephrase's 27,104 files) and in
_HASH_HOLDOUT, and it uses _fmt_finephrase like the other two.

>>> LAUNCH PROCEDURE -- READ THIS, THE OBVIOUS COMMAND IS WRONG <<<
Passing "--load checkpoint/pp1_bvocab_e10s.pt" to longrun.sh DOES NOT WORK, and
fails in a way that looks fine for hours before quietly stopping short:
  * vla.py sets _start_step from the loaded checkpoint (vla.py:955) and starts
    the step counter there, so k1's own checkpoints carry pp1's ~400,000 offset.
  * longrun computes REM = TOTAL - ckpt_step(checkpoint/$M.pt).  On the FIRST
    invocation checkpoint/k1_*.pt does not exist, so DONE=0 and it asks for the
    full TOTAL of NEW steps -- but after any crash DONE is ~400,000+, and with
    TOTAL=400,000 that is REM<=0, i.e. "COMPLETE" without training anything.
    The two invocations disagree about what the number means.
INSTEAD, SEED THE NEW MNEMONIC'S CHECKPOINT AND LET longrun's OWN RESUME LOGIC
DO IT.  That makes DONE correct on every invocation, and longrun adds the --load
itself:
    PP1=$(python -c "import torch;print(torch.load('checkpoint/pp1_bvocab_e10s.pt',\
map_location='cpu',weights_only=False)['step'])")
    cp checkpoint/pp1_bvocab_e10s.pt checkpoint/k1_know80_100m.pt
    ./exp/launch.sh exp/longrun.sh k1_know80_100m $((PP1 + 400000)) 20 \
        --mix cosmo:0.30,fpfaq:0.30,fptut:0.20,swiki:0.12,fwedu:0.08 \
        --dataset cosmo --streaming \
        --eval_interval 20000 --eval_corpora cosmo,swiki,web
  TOTAL is an ABSOLUTE step number (pp1's final step + 400,000 new), not a count
  of new steps.  400,000 new steps = 100M tokens = ~37 h at 3.03 steps/s.

  --eval_corpora cosmo,swiki,web: one in-mix AI corpus, one in-mix human corpus,
  and web as the CROSS-DOMAIN anchor that every number in PLAN.txt's reference
  table is measured on.  (The eval-corpus renderer bug that silently dropped
  'pile' from c1 for its entire run was fixed 2026-08-28; this run is the first
  to exercise a multi-corpus eval since.)

  NOTE ON THE LOG: vla.py prepends the loaded checkpoint's embedded log, so
  log/exp.k1_know80_100m will OPEN WITH ALL OF pp1's HISTORY and chart.py will
  plot both curriculum stages as one continuous curve.  That is the desired
  reading here, but it means k1's log is not a record of k1 alone.

K1 AMENDMENT  2026-08-29 -- mix reworked before launch (still not started)
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
The first K1 draft above is SUPERSEDED. Three changes, all user decisions:

1. tiny was rejected (low entropy) -- already recorded above.
2. MATH IS NOW ALLOWED.  The "no math, no code" rule was stage-2 policy; it has
   been relaxed. fpmath re-enters at a deliberately small 0.07 so math is
   INTRODUCED rather than dumped in; code (pile/brt/s1k) is still out.
3. cosmo IS NO LONGER FILTERED and a BROAD config is added.

WHY cosmoweb.  Asked what else cosmopedia offers, the answer is that 'stories'
is a genre, not a distribution.  Measured over the corpus:
    config           examples      size   character
    web_samples_v1  12,426,348   69.1 GB  open-web seeded -- BROAD
    web_samples_v2  10,345,867   58.7 GB  open-web seeded -- BROAD
    stories          4,992,964   21.3 GB  narrative + reddit/forum register
    auto_math_text   1,949,895    8.8 GB  math only
    stanford         1,020,024    6.3 GB  textbook narrative
    wikihow            179,191    0.9 GB  one format, overlaps fptut
    openstax           126,332    0.7 GB  college textbook
    khanacademy         24,123    0.1 GB  one format, too small to matter
  The two web_samples configs are 74% of all Cosmopedia and the only ones seeded
  from general web content rather than one source type.  v2 internally spans
  general 73% / college_students 27% and blogpost 62% / textbook_academic 27% /
  textbook_narrative 5% / wikihow 5% -- genuinely mixed register, open domain.
  v2 (the refined seeding) is registered as 'cosmoweb'.  It is 58.7 GB: ALWAYS
  --streaming.  At 0.35 of 100M tokens it never wraps, so those are 35M DISTINCT
  tokens rather than repeats.

WHY THE cosmo FILTER WENT.  _fmt_cosmo kept only audience == 'young_children'
(26.3% of rows -- the same rows format == 'story_children' selects).  It is
deleted, so cosmo now reads its text column like any other corpus and carries the
'general' register (73.7%, median grade 11.5 vs 9.4) and the reddit/forums
formats.  BACKWARD COMPATIBILITY IS DELIBERATELY BROKEN: every cosmo number in
this file from BEFORE 2026-08-29 -- f1_grade5_1b, c1_sense_1b and their EVAL
lines -- was measured on the young_children subset and must NOT be compared to
anything measured after.  The user accepted this explicitly.

THE MIX AS IT WILL RUN -- 80% AI / 20% HUMAN:
    AI     cosmoweb 0.35  cosmopedia web_samples_v2 (Mixtral) -- BROAD general
           fpfaq    0.15  finephrase/faq (SmolLM2) -- explanatory Q&A
           fptut    0.15  finephrase/tutorial (SmolLM2) -- how-to / pop-science
           cosmo    0.08  cosmopedia/stories UNFILTERED (Mixtral) -- narrative
                          and forum register
           fpmath   0.07  finephrase/math (SmolLM2) -- math, small on purpose
    HUMAN  swiki    0.12  Simple English Wikipedia
           fwedu    0.08  fineweb-edu (human web, classifier-selected)
  Smoke-tested on CPU: all seven corpora resolve and train.

HANDOVER CRITERION for pp1 -> k1, and two tools built to measure it  2026-08-29
--------------------------------------------------------------------------------
k1 starts from pp1's weights, so WHEN pp1 IS STOPPED IS A DESIGN DECISION, not a
convenience. The rule chosen by the user: stop at the KNEE of the loss curve, and
only if free-running GEN text is COHERENT. Neither is a thing to judge by eye, so
both are now measured.

exp/knee.py   -- bins the loss curve, medians each bin (a mean lets one spike move
  a slope), takes slope per 10k steps, smooths, and calls a knee where |slope| has
  dropped below --frac of the post-warmup PEAK and STAYS there --persist bins.
  Persistence is the load-bearing part; see the b1 result below.
exp/coherence.py -- scores GEN text by how surprised a FROZEN reference model
  (pythia-70m, unrelated to the model under test, so the run cannot game it) is by
  it, in nats/token, placed on a 0-100% scale between two anchors computed from
  the run's own corpus: real corpus lines (100%) and the same lines with words
  SHUFFLED (0%). Fluent-looking word salad scores near 0 by construction.
exp/verbatim.py -- longest run of consecutive words appearing verbatim in the
  corpus, plus coverage by runs >= N words. Word runs, not character overlap or
  edit distance: a model that has learned only the STYLE scores high on those
  while reproducing nothing.

CALIBRATION -- the two corpus regimes behave completely differently:

  d1 (wdef, 466K entries, ~3 passes -- effectively infinite at our budget)
    slope decays MONOTONICALLY: 100% -> 54 -> 38 -> 30 -> 25 -> 21 -> 18 -> 16 -> 14%
    KNEE at step 151,990 (epoch ~2). Textbook diminishing returns.

  b1 (bdict e3, 848 entries, 536 passes -- tiny and repeated)
    slope FELL AND THEN CAME BACK: 100% (ep 181) -> 78.6% (ep 228) -> 80.4 -> 84.4
    -> 87.6 -> 92.5 -> 96.6 -> 98.6 -> 99.6% (ep 536).  NO KNEE.
    The mid-run flattening around epoch 230 is a FALSE PLATEAU; the descent
    RE-ACCELERATES as memorization takes over. A knee detector without the
    persistence requirement would have fired there and stopped the run at exactly
    the wrong moment. pp1 is also small and repeated, so EXPECT THIS SHAPE.

  AND THE RESULT THAT CHANGES THE EXPECTATION -- b1 at 536 epochs:
    coherence  74.6% (null prompt), 39-59% (keyed prompts)
    verbatim   longest run 4 words, coverage 0.0%
    "var: The wagon got off by gold in the morning. All of the game is visiting
     the ad show."
  Grammatical, corpus-flavoured, and NOT A SINGLE MEMORIZED SENTENCE after 536
  passes over 848 documents. COHERENCE ARRIVES LONG BEFORE VERBATIM RECALL, and
  on this architecture recall may not arrive at all within budget. The earlier
  expectation for pp1 -- "eventually we should see verbatim text from the training
  set" -- is now in doubt, and that is itself the interesting finding: a 30.4M
  parameter recurrent CNN with a one-token gradient horizon generalizes where a
  transformer of this size would be expected to memorize.

  pp1 at step 6,000 (epoch ~16) for reference: coherence 34-53%, longest verbatim
  run 1-3 words. Nowhere near either bar.

PROPOSED STOP RULE (evidence-based, not arbitrary):
    exp/knee.py fires (|slope| < 25% of peak, 3 consecutive bins), AND
    exp/coherence.py null-prompt score >= 70% sustained over 3 samples
  70% is b1's demonstrated 74.6%, i.e. a bar this architecture is known to clear
  on this kind of corpus. If the knee never fires -- the b1 outcome -- then the
  handover is a JUDGEMENT about diminishing returns per GPU-hour, and it should be
  made explicitly and written down here, not by letting the run hit --run_steps.

================================================================================
        PP1 RESULT -- THE MEMORIZATION TRANSITION, AND A RETRACTED PREDICTION
                    stopped 2026-08-30 as the baseline
================================================================================
Stopped at step 315,000 = 845 epochs over 8,480 one-sentence documents, train
loss 0.933.  Weights kept as checkpoint/pp1_bvocab_e10s.BASELINE_315000.pt.
Every 10,000-step stash is also on disk, so any point of the curve is
re-examinable.

THE RESULT.  Free-running generation went from reproducing NOTHING of the corpus
to reproducing it wholesale, and the transition was abrupt:

    epoch 187    6-word run, isolated, and on the corpus's most repeated stem
    epoch 425    7-word run, single-occurrence sentence
    epoch 535   10-word run, 1 hit in 20 samples
    epoch 602   10-word run, 11 hits in 25 samples, FIVE of them 100% verbatim
                "she asked the driver to stop at the next corner"  <- entire
                sample, matching entry 'stop' exactly

  Coverage across 25 consecutive samples at epoch 602:
    0 0 0 0 100 88 0 62 0 0 0 75 67 100 70 0 42 0 0 100 0 0 0 100 100
  Flat at zero for 500+ epochs, then 44% of samples in the next 60K steps.

I PREDICTED THE OPPOSITE AND WAS WRONG, and the error is worth recording because
it was an inference from one run to another.  b1 (the keyed e3 corpus) reached
536 epochs with 74.6% coherence and 0.0% verbatim coverage, and I concluded from
that this architecture "learns structure and does not memorize", and that pp1's
stated success criterion would not be met.  b1 had simply not run long enough:
pp1 crossed over between epoch 535 and 602.  A negative result at epoch 536 said
nothing about epoch 602, and I treated it as if it did.

THE OTHER PREDICTION THAT INVERTED.  From b1's slope RE-ACCELERATING (78.6% ->
99.6% of peak) I expected pp1's knee never to fire and coherence to be the gate.
Exactly backwards: the knee FIRED at step 155,990 (epoch 416, seven consecutive
bins under 25% of peak), while coherence plateaued at 32-54% from step 31,000
onward and never approached the 70% bar.  Loss fell 2.46 -> 1.79 across a window
where sentence-level coherence did not move at all -- local prediction improving
while whole-sentence quality stood still.

MEASUREMENT NOTE.  Milestone-to-milestone coherence readings on 12 samples are
NOISE: the sd of a 20-sample mean is 7.3%, so nothing under ~15 points is real.
I reported a "clear upward trend" from 43.9% -> 56.1% that scoring all 230
samples showed to be a plateau.  Score the whole log, not the tail.

================================================================================
      V1  v1_vocab_mix90  -- BATCHWISE VOCABULARY STREAM + LONG-FORM CORPUS
                     started 2026-08-30, FROM RANDOM WEIGHTS
================================================================================
NOT a continuation of pp1.  Fresh initialisation (no --load; step 0 opens at loss
10.906 / grad 647, the untrained signature every run in this file starts from).
pp1 is the BASELINE to compare against, not the starting point.

THE DATA -- two authored corpora, both exactly 10,000 single sentences:
  vocab_phase1.txt   the 848 grade 1-5 words.  basic_dict_e10.txt split into
                     sentences (8,480), deduped to 8,440, then 1,560 authored
                     additions: 40 to restore words dedup had pushed below 10,
                     and +2 each to the first 760 words.  basic_vocab.txt is in
                     GRADE ORDER, so the first 760 are the most elementary and
                     frequent -- they get 12 examples, the rest 10.
  vocab_phase2.txt   5,000 NEW words x 2 sentences in genuinely different
                     contexts (different situation, and a different SENSE where
                     the word has one: bail legal/maritime, crane machine/bird,
                     acid chemical/remark, vice failing/tool).
  Both verified line by line through exp/sentsplit.py -- the canonical copy of
  vla.py's splitter, which asserts it reproduces vla.py's own counts -- so every
  line is one sentence by exactly the rule --bdict_split applies.  All unique,
  ASCII, and zero word-pairs share >=4 content words (no near-duplicate framing).

HOW THE PHASE-2 VOCABULARY WAS BUILT, AND THE APPROACH THAT FAILED FIRST.
  FAILED: rank corpus word-frequency, keep real dictionary headwords, drop
  basic_vocab.  It produced non-words and junk -- over-stripped lemmas ('foo'
  from food, 'numb' from number, 'coul' from could, 'dure' from during, because
  the 466K-headword dictionary contains obscure lowercase entries and the rule
  took the SHORTEST reachable one), 800 duplicate entries, proper nouns from
  Wikipedia (american, latvia, bonaparte, vermont), and inflections of excluded
  words (used/based/called).  Rejected by the user: "too many non-words".
  WORKED: authored by 14 topic-domain agents (450 words each, excluding
  basic_vocab AND each other's words), giving 6,300 -> 5,843 after dedup and
  inflection-collapse, then trimmed to 5,000 by four independent reviewers.
  A SECOND FAILURE ON THE WAY: I tried to do that trim with corpus frequency and
  it kept gneiss/exosphere/palisade/antithesis while discarding kiosk/detour/
  gorgeous/restrain.  The corpus is FAQ+Simple-Wikipedia, so it measures TOPIC
  COVERAGE IN AN ENCYCLOPEDIA, not commonness in English -- the same category of
  error as the first attempt.  Judgement is the right instrument for "is this
  word common"; a corpus is only the right instrument for "does this word
  appear here".  The four reviewers converged independently on the same cuts
  (instruments, narrow species, construction jargon, archaic terms, regional
  usage, formal-abstraction filler), which is the evidence the line is right.

  Sentences were generated by HAIKU on a deliberately SHORT prompt: the diverse-
  context requirement stated once with one example, and nothing else.  Format was
  moved OUT of the prompt and into a validator.  Result: 9,981 of 10,000 clean on
  the first pass -- a better rate than Sonnet achieved on the 9-rule prompt used
  for basic_dict_e10.  A long constraint list makes a small model worse at the
  one thing only the model can do; the rest is a script's job.

THE BATCHWISE MIX -- why not --mix:
    --vocab_files vocab_phase1.txt,vocab_phase2.txt --vocab_slots 225
    --dataset cosmoweb --streaming
  225 of the 250 batch slots are _VocabSlot, the other 25 are ordinary framed
  _ClipSlots.  EVERY SLOT CONTRIBUTES EXACTLY ONE TOKEN PER STEP, so the slot
  split IS the token split: 225/250 is exactly 90% / 10%, with no dependence on
  document length.  --mix cannot do this -- its token-proportional sampling holds
  only on average, and only via a running estimate of each corpus's mean clip
  length.
  _VocabSlot differs from _ClipSlot in exactly two ways, both deliberate:
    NO FRAMING     no <STX>/<ETX>; sentences concatenate head to tail, so the
                   (x,y) pair straddling a boundary is a real transition from one
                   sentence's last token to the next one's first.
    NEVER RESETS   is_start() is always False, so the recurrent state is never
                   cleared on those slots.  A corpus slot resets at every <ETX>;
                   these run unbroken for the whole run.
  Each of the 225 holds its OWN shuffled permutation, round-robined across the
  two files, so phase1/phase2 split 113/112 and the 225 slots are 225 independent
  orderings rather than 225 copies of one.

  EPOCH COLUMN TRACKS THE VOCAB FILES ONLY.  _epoch_num() takes the minimum
  across datasets, so an effectively-infinite corpus would pin it at 1 forever;
  corpus epochs are suppressed whenever vocab slots are active.  The reported
  epoch is total sentences drawn / file size, which is the honest reading when
  225 slots each hold their own permutation.

  LONG-FORM CORPUS: cosmoweb (cosmopedia web_samples_v2).  Chosen to MATCH the
  vocabulary content: it is AI-generated, long-form, and open-web-seeded across
  blogpost 62% / textbook_academic 27% / textbook_narrative 5%, which is the
  closest available span to phase 2's general-knowledge domains.  At 58.7 GB it
  never repeats, so the 10% is 40M distinct tokens over a 400K-step run.

  WHAT TO WATCH: the model sees a document boundary on only 1 slot in 10.  Every
  earlier run in this file reset its state constantly; this one mostly does not.
  Whether the state stays healthy without regular resets is the open question,
  and dff_std is the number to watch.
